- **#151** *(flight-sw)* I/O RESET CANNOT CLEAR A FAIL-TO-SYNC VOTE OR THE CAM, and neither can an OPS mode recall on its own. DIR_IORESET, the program scheduled when the I/O RESET key is depressed, resets only strings and buses: FC strings 1-4, launch data buses 12 and 13, payload string 5, plus a PMU reset, a GPCF reset and an error counter. It never touches redundant-set membership or the fail discretes. From a good GPC it resets only the buses that GPC's set commands, and a voted-out GPC is no longer in that set; from the failed GPC it resets only what that GPC still commands, which after a vote-out is nothing. What clears the vote is a redundant-set re-formation: ARC_GPC_RECONFIG writes CZ2B_RS, distributes it over the ICC, calls SMR(ARC_SYNC) to form the RS, and then sets ARC_CAM to 0.
  - evidence: Read 2026-09-16 from OI340600/SSSRC/DIRIOR.hal, whose header states that it requests FCOS reset all strings and buses commanded by the requesting GPC or by the redundant set of which it is a member; DIR_BUS_MASK is built from CZ2B_BUS_MASK1/2 indexed by TFCMID and RECONFIG is called per bus, with mass memory buses 18 and 19 explicitly excluded. ARC_CAM=0 sits at ARCGPC.hal line 035210BL immediately after SMR(ARC_SYNC) at 035200BI. ARC_GPC_RECONFIG is scheduled from DM6OPS.hal:403 and :447 (OPS request and MODE RECALL from a DM1 keyboard), DM5NEW.hal:476, ARGREC.hal:146 and ARFDPSCO.hal:509. ITEM 47 on the GPC MEMORY display schedules it directly but is locked out with CDLK_K6 unless the entering GPC is in no redundant set and is in OPS 0, so it cannot be used from a set member. USER CONFIRMED EMPIRICALLY the same day: I/O RESET EXEC from both a good GPC and the failed GPC left the lockout in place, and OPS 201 PRO from two different keyboards did not clear it either.
  - csects `DIRIOR,DIR_IORESET,ARCGPC,ARC_GPC_RECONFIG,DM6OPS,DM5NEW,ARGREC,ARFDPSCO,FCMSFAIL,FCMCSYNC` &middot; tape `v44boot` &middot; files `SSSRC/DIRIOR.hal,SSSRC/ARCGPC.hal,SSSRC/DM6OPS.hal,SSSRC/ARFDPSCO.hal,SSSRC/CD0001.dfg` &middot; symptom `I/O RESET does nothing,I/O RESET flashing,CAM stays lit,fail-to-sync lockout will not clear,voted out GPC will not rejoin` &middot; doc `OI340600 SSSRC/DIRIOR.hal header; ARCGPC.hal FORM RS`
- **#153** *(emulator)* REDUNDANT-SET MEMBERS DO NOT PERFORM IDENTICAL I/O IN OUR MODEL, and each extra I/O is a synchronising SVC that one computer executes ALONE -- a handshake the other three must reach within FCOS's 3.85 ms or vote it out. This is the root cause of the IPR-handshake slip of #148/#149, and it is chronic rather than a rare timing accident. It is NOT host scheduling: the simulated-time barrier holds all four machines within 25 us of each other, so a computer that burns 3.85 ms of simulated time spinning in FCMSSYNC proves its peers executed ~3.8 ms of simulated work in the same window -- they were running, not descheduled. It is also not barrier tuning, which #125 already settled. New evidence for #137: the asymmetry is in peripheral I/O between set members and non-members sharing buses with no arbitration.
  - evidence: Measured 2026-09-16 over 208.6 s of healthy running immediately before the failure, from the YAGPC_SYNCTRACE log of a four-GPC --bce-network run. Codes issued per computer: timer 9126/9126/9126/9126, SSIP 1304/1304/1304/1304 and IPR 870/870/870/870 match EXACTLY across all four, while the I/O-derived codes do not -- SVC 33901 on GPC1 against 33887/33885/33887, and I/O complete 18581 against 18580/18579/18579. So the divergence is sourced specifically in I/O, not general drift: GPC1 runs about one extra synchronising SVC every 14 seconds. Join delay from the first computer issuing a code to the last joining it: SVC median 75 us, 99% 727 us, MAX 3645 us against a 3850 us deadline -- a 5 percent margin, with I/O complete max 2648, timer 2515 and SSIP 2285. One lone handshake every 14 s against a tail already touching the deadline makes a vote-out a matter of time, which matches hours of good running before a loss. The failing round itself: GPC1 alone issued 100 SVC at t=794931.472804, no peer joined, GPC1 dropped to 111 null at .477193 after 4.39 ms, and GPC2/3/4 raised 010 IPR at .477466-.477483 and voted. A CANDIDATE MECHANISM FOR THE TAIL, from the barrier statistics of a 22-minute four-GPC run (--gpcs 1,2,3,4 --bce-network, delta 25 us): 'simulated-time barrier held 158,907,466 times, 1090.851 s total, 0 abandoned; 154,462,371 released while spinning, 666,322 SLEEPS'. Nothing was abandoned, so the barrier kept its guarantee -- but two thirds of a million holds were NOT cleared by the 200 us spin and went to pthread_cond_timedwait with a BARRIER_SLEEP_SEC = 2 ms timeout. A machine asleep there is stopped for milliseconds, which is the scale of the measured join tail (SVC max 3645 us against the 3850 us deadline) and far above the 67-87 us median. This would explain why #125's sweep of YAGPC_BARRIER_US did not help: the damage would not be how far the machines DRIFT but what it costs one to be STOPPED while a handshake is open. TO TEST: correlate barrier sleeps against the join delays in a YAGPC_SYNCTRACE run, or instrument the sleep path to report when a machine sleeps with a non-null code on its own output lines. REEVALUATED 2026-09-16 LATE, AND THE UNITS WERE WRONG. discretes_synctrace prints yagpc_monotonic_seconds() -- WALL CLOCK -- while FCOS's 3.85 ms timeout is counted by a spin loop and is therefore SIMULATED time. Every join-delay figure above mixes the two, so the framing 'the tail reaches 3645 us against a 3850 us deadline, a 5% margin' IS NOT ESTABLISHED: a large wall-clock gap can be wholly benign, because a machine held at the barrier has its simulated clock stopped too and spends none of its budget. The same objection kills a barrier-sleep hypothesis raised the same evening (666,322 sleeps of up to 2 ms): a sleep STOPS simulated time and so cannot consume the timeout. BUT THE BARRIER'S OWN GUARANTEE NOW ARGUES THE OTHER WAY, and more strongly. Delta is 25 us and the run reported 0 ABANDONED over 22 minutes, so the bound never lapsed; therefore for a victim to burn 3.85 ms of SIMULATED time spinning in FCMSSYNC, its peers must have advanced ~3.83 ms of simulated time as well. They were executing real work and never reached the sync point. So the cause is EXECUTION-PATH DIVERGENCE, not timing, and the barrier is exonerated rather than implicated. THIS ALSO RETIRES THE 'SECOND MECHANISM': GPC3's failure at a 101 timer sync was set aside because timer counts matched exactly across all four computers, but equal TOTALS over 208 s do not make each ROUND symmetric, and extra I/O work shifts WHEN a machine reaches its next sync point. Divergence sourced in I/O will surface at whichever handshake comes next, SVC or timer. ONE CAUSE, NOT TWO. FIRST THING TO DO: put the machine's simulated time on the SYNC trace line beside the wall clock -- everything here is inference from a measurement in the wrong units, and with simulated time in the trace 'did the peers really execute 3.8 ms of work' becomes a direct observation instead of an argument.
  - csects `FCMSSYNC,FCMISYNC,FCMSFAIL,FCMCSYNC` &middot; procs `DK6,BCE6,discretes` &middot; tape `v44boot` &middot; env `YAGPC_SYNCTRACE,YAGPC_BARRIER_US` &middot; files `src/run.c,src/vehicle.c,src/bcenet_transport.c` &middot; symptom `lone SVC handshake,fail to sync after hours,IPR handshake slip,GPC voted out at random,join delay 3645 us` &middot; tsec `794700-794932`
- **#157** *(flight-sw)* AN OVERLAY IS LOADED GPC-TO-GPC BY PREFERENCE, OVER THE LAUNCH DATA BUSES, AND ONLY FROM MASS MEMORY AS A FALLBACK. ARCGPC's source selector is explicit: MEM_SRC=1 major-function overlay GPC TO GPC, 2 program overlay GPC TO GPC, 3 MF overlay FROM MM, 4 PG overlay FROM MM, and the search runs DO FOR MEM_SRC = 1 TO 4 -- GPC first, mass memory last. The transfer moves on the LAUNCH DATA BUSES 12 and 13, not the mass memory buses: for MEM_SRC < 3 the code sets ARC_BUS_DELTA = -6 with the comment 'LDB BUS 12,13 -- MM BUS 18,19', so a source GPC commands the LDB and plays the mass memory unit's part while the receiving computers listen. It is gated on ARC_DPS_STATUS$(3), the GPC/LDB item on the DPS UTILITY SPEC (ITEM 11, per the DR105712 comment), and the source GPC is GIVEN the bus on demand -- 'ASSIGN LDB BUS TO SOURCE GPCS IF REQUIRED', by ICC message type 3 -- which is why both CONFIG 2 and CONFIG 3 leave L1 and L2 unassigned in the NBAT. CONSEQUENCE: A COMPUTER WITH NO MASS MEMORY ASSIGNMENT IS NOT STUCK. It does not need one to be reloaded; it needs the LDB path and the ICC message that sets it up. Any reasoning of the form 'GPC3 has no MM in the NBAT, therefore it cannot reload an OPS overlay' is WRONG -- that argument was made and retracted the same evening.
  - evidence: Read 2026-09-16 from OI340600/SSSRC/ARCGPC.hal: the MEM_SRC legend at 054100BI-054400BI; the search loop 'DO FOR MEM_SRC = 1 TO 4' at 038369CV; ARC_SRC_GPC$(MEM_SRC) at 039220BY and GPC_OVERLAY_SOURCE(MEM_SRC,ARC_J) at 062100BY; bus selection at 055500BL-056500BI where ARC_BUS_ID is 18 or 19 and, for MEM_SRC<3 with ARC_DPS_STATUS$(3) ON, ARC_BUS_DELTA=-6 'LDB BUS 12,13 -- MM BUS 18,19'; the dynamic assignment at 058502BQ-058570BP, 'ASSIGN LDB BUS TO SOURCE GPCS IF REQUIRED', using GTG_OVERLAY_SOURCE and TFCM_LDB_CMDR and sending ICC_MSG_TYPE = 3 when the source GPC does not already command the bus. Corroborated by DM6OPS's MODE RECALL comment, 'NO MMU PRE POSITION WILL ACTUALLY TAKE PLACE SINCE AT LEAST ONE GPC MUST HAVE THE MF/OPS OVERLAYS'. NOT YET TESTED IN OUR MODEL: buses 12 and 13 are inside YAGPC_BUS_MAX = 24 and nothing treats them specially, so under --bce-network they would ride the framer like any other bus -- but whether one GPC's LDB transmission reaches the other computers' framers has never been checked, and neither has the ICC message that assigns the bus (see #107). THE CREW PROCEDURE HAS A NAME: FREEZE DRY (FD). A spare computer is loaded with a memory configuration it is not running, so that it HOLDS that OPS's overlays and can supply them to others -- the caching GPC. From STS-134 Post Insertion book page 1-2, step 3 'LOAD MC 2 INTO G2FD GPC': PL, GPC/CRT <FD GPC>/X EXEC; on X: PL GPC MEMORY; CONFIG - ITEM 45 +2 EXEC; GPC - ITEM 46 +<FD GPC> EXEC; STORE - ITEM 47 EXEC; 'Store complete when MC = 02'. The same book uses 'Retry GPC FREEZE DRY (step 3)' and names the roles G2, G2FD, FD and SM in its single-PASS-GPC-failure matrix. ARFDPSCO.hal gives the rules: ITEM 45 sets CDJV_FD_MC and is refused (CDLK_K6) unless CZ2V_GRT_MC$(ARF_I;) = ARF_I; ITEM 46 sets CDJV_FD_GPC and is refused unless CZ2B_RS$(TFCMID;) = HEX'0000' AND ARF_I = TFCMID -- so it must be typed on a computer that is NOT IN A REDUNDANT SET, NAMING ITSELF; ITEM 47 sets CZ2V_REC_XERR = 2 and schedules ARC_GPC_RECONFIG, and is itself locked out if the entering GPC is in a redundant set or not in OPS 0. CD0001.dfg declares them ITEM=(45,UE,I2,1,10), ITEM=(46,UE,I1,1,5), ITEM=(47,UX). SO THERE ARE TWO PROCEDURES, AND THEY FIT TOGETHER: freeze dry PUTS the overlays into a spare computer (crew action, ITEMs 45/46/47 on its own GPC MEMORY display, which it can only have if it has been given a CRT); and at the next OPS transition ARCGPC's MEM_SRC 1/2 search TAKES them from whichever GPC holds them, over LDB 12/13, in preference to mass memory.
  - csects `ARCGPC,ARC_MEM_OVL,ARC_OVERLAY_MASKS,GTG_OVERLAY_SOURCE,DM6OPS,DM2APP,DM5NEW,ARFDPSCO` &middot; procs `BCE12,BCE13,BCE18,BCE19,ICC,LDB` &middot; tape `v44boot` &middot; files `SSSRC/ARCGPC.hal,SSSRC/DM6OPS.hal,src/bcenet_transport.c,src/vehicle.c` &middot; symptom `cannot reload overlay,no mass memory bus,OPS overlay,GPC to GPC transfer,launch data bus,pre position,settles into OPS 0,freeze dry,FD GPC,ITEM 45,ITEM 46,ITEM 47` &middot; doc `STS-134 Post Insertion p.1-2 step 3 (LOAD MC INTO FD GPC) and the MEMORY CONFIGURATION TABLE p.1-3; OI340600 ARCGPC.hal MEM_SRC legend; ARFDPSCO.hal items 45-47`
- **#161** *(method)* PERFORMANCE NUMBERS TAKEN ON THE 20-CORE HOST ON 2026-09-17 ARE NOT TRUSTWORTHY, because its kernel had oopsed four times in AppArmor, whose hooks sit on every socket call in yaGPC2's hot path. Functional gate results from the same period ARE trustworthy -- they score deterministic event counts, not timings. WHAT CAUSED THE OOPS IS NOT ESTABLISHED: this entry first blamed the failing disk, and that link does not hold up.
  - evidence: dmesg: 'BUG: unable to handle page fault for address: ffff9b1601789053', RIP common_perm_cond+0x6d/0x200 (AppArmor), Oops #1 at uptime 660700, #2 692208, #3 778629, #4 865034 -- the SAME faulting address every time, each raised by systemd-tmpfiles doing statx. /proc/sys/kernel/tainted = 12417 = P(1) + D(128, the kernel died) + OE(4096) + unsigned(8192). WHY THE DISK EXPLANATION WAS WRONG, per the user 2026-09-17: AppArmor's labels and per-inode security blobs are KERNEL MEMORY, and the faulting address is a kernel pointer loaded from an object and dereferenced at +0x53 -- not file content off a platter. ext4 answers a corrupt on-disk inode with an I/O error, not a wild pointer. And systemd-tmpfiles manages about 134 paths of which 133 are on the ROOT volume (/var /run /etc /dev /tmp /root); only one names /home, the symlink onto the storage volume. What existed was a CORRELATION -- one machine with both a failing disk and an oopsing kernel -- and the mechanism joining them was invented, not measured. The recurrence at one address fits a persistent corrupted kernel object or an AppArmor use-after-free at least as well, and neither needs a disk fault. COUNTER-EXAMPLE, same day: the 2011 i7-3930K showed Xorg spinning with the identical signature (98% of a core, state R, wchan 0, ~8 voluntary context switches a second against ~456 when healthy) with ZERO oopses, taint 12289 lacking the D bit, and no disk fault -- so the X spin does not require the kernel fault it was attributed to. INDEPENDENTLY TRUE AND WORTH KEEPING: a saturated four-GPC profile puts aa_inet_msg_perm at 1.41% and apparmor_socket_recvmsg at 0.85% of cycles, both on the per-syscall path being optimised, so 3%-resolution work should not be done on a kernel in that state whatever put it there.
  - env `YAGPC_PACETRACE` &middot; files `src/bcenet_transport.c,src/discretes.c,src/envcache.c` &middot; symptom `Xorg 100%,kernel oops,apparmor,measurement contaminated`
- **#164** *(method)* MULTI-GPC IPL FAILS ON A SLOW HOST BY RUNNING OUT OF WALL-CLOCK TIME, NOT BY LOSING SYNC -- and the per-GPC procedure's own wait timeouts were the hard stop. A crew script's times are WALL CLOCK while the computers it drives run on SIMULATED time, so every fixed delay and every timeout shrinks in simulated terms in proportion to how far below rate 1.0 the host is.
  - evidence: On a 2011 i7-3930K (6 cores, no AVX2) a single GPC driving three displays runs at rate 0.18-0.30, about a quarter of real time, against 1.00 on the 20-core machine. Measured per-computer IPL cost here, from panelO6's own wait reports: 'wait crt 1 title GPCIPL' met after 41.2 s, 'wait gpc 1 mode-tb RUN' met after 163.8 s with nothing else competing; the second computer's GPCIPL menu took 65.5 s because the first was by then taking its share. Four computers plus the 14-item NBAT at +8 s each, OPS 301 and the IDP3 block come to over 1250 s, so 480 s reached 1 of 4 in RUN and 1200 s could not have finished either. THE HARD STOP was ipl-one-gpc.script's 'timeout 300' on the RUN talkback: the first computer needed 164 s of it unopposed, and each later computer is slower again, so the fourth would have been cut off and the script would have carried on issuing crew actions to a computer that never came up. Raised to 300/900, which costs a fast host nothing because a wait ends when its condition is met. WHAT IS NOT WRONG, checked across three runs on this host: zero BCE receive timeouts, zero error terminations, zero fail-votes, zero fail-to-sync. The emulator's own bus timeouts are safe on a slow host because iop.c:1991 compares against 'now = iop->cpu->elapsedTimeUs', which is SIMULATED time, not the wall clock. The computers that did come up ran clean.
  - env `YAGPC_PACETRACE` &middot; files `discretePanel/examples/ipl-one-gpc.script,discretePanel/examples/4gpc-g3-startup.script,src/iop.c` &middot; symptom `multi-GPC will not IPL,1 of 4 in RUN,wait timeout,slow host`
- **#165** *(method)* WHY FOUR-GPC RUNS ONLY EVER GOT ONE OR TWO COMPUTERS INTO RUN, and it is not a sync failure: the per-GPC IPL procedure waits for the GPCIPL menu with 'wait crt N title GPCIPL', which is a STATE test, on a CRT that three of the four computers share in turn. The previous computer's menu is still on that screen, so from the second sharer onward the wait is met instantly, the script types ITEM 1 EXEC into a computer that has not yet put up its menu, the menu-selected load never starts, and that computer sits until its RUN wait expires.
  - evidence: crewscript.py _poll_screen: with a title, met = 'the text is CONTAINED in the page now on show' -- nothing requires the page to be NEW. panelO6's own log is explicit, from a 2100 s four-GPC run on 2026-09-17: 'wait met after 65.9 s: wait crt 2 title GPCIPL' for GPC2, which had a clean CRT2, then 'wait met after 0.0 s: wait crt 2 title GPCIPL  [crt2: GPC MEMORY 27 OPTION _ (1) 2 GPCIPL MENU]' for GPC3, matching GPC2's leftover page. 4gpc-g3-startup.script gives crt=2 to GPC2, GPC3 AND GPC4, and GPC3 and GPC4 are exactly the two that never came up, in three separate runs. THE FIX is the transition test the vocabulary already has: 'wait crt  new-screen' before the title wait. new-screen baselines the page when the wait begins and returns only when a DIFFERENT one arrives, so the stale page must go first. A LONGER FIXED DELAY WOULD NOT HAVE FIXED IT and would have hidden it on a fast host: the IDP reload gets a few seconds of WALL clock to clear the display, which was many display polls on the 20-core machine and about one simulated second at rate 0.24 -- the same wall-versus-simulated trap as #164. WHAT WAS NOT WRONG: across every run on this host, zero fail-votes, zero fail-to-sync, zero BCE receive timeouts, zero error terminations, and the three computers that were running showed instantaneous rates of 0.244/0.244/0.244, identical to three decimals, which is the simulated-time barrier holding them in step exactly as designed.
  - files `discretePanel/examples/ipl-one-gpc.script,discretePanel/crewscript.py` &middot; symptom `1 of 4 in RUN,2 of 4 in RUN,GPC3 never runs,GPC4 never runs,stale display,garbage on CRT2`
- **#168** *(emulator)* ON THIS HOST the emulation loop is MEMORY-LATENCY-BOUND, not work-bound, so removing instructions from it does not make it faster; only costs that STALL -- syscalls above all -- are worth attacking. CAUTION ON SCOPE: this is measured on a 2011 Sandy Bridge-E (i7-3930K, 256 KB L2 per core, no AVX2) and is NOT a permanent property of the emulator. A machine with a larger cache, better prefetch or more memory-level parallelism could shift the balance back towards work, so re-measure IPC and cache-miss rate on new hardware before concluding that instruction-path work is pointless there.
  - evidence: perf stat on the emulation thread of a one-GPC run, 2026-09-17, while it sat at 98.3% CPU in state R: IPC 0.82 against a core that can retire about 4; frontend stalled 58.58% of cycles; cache-misses 74.74% OF ALL LAST-LEVEL CACHE REFERENCES -- 205M misses in 20 s, about 10M trips to RAM per second, which at ~100 ns each is the whole time budget; branch-misses only 1.14%, so branches are NOT the problem. WHAT THIS PREDICTS AND WHAT WAS MEASURED, uninstrumented 1-GPC, 600 s, after the display-buffer fix in #167 brought run-to-run spread down to 0.2%: attend-only baseline 697010 steps/s; plus a 64 KB hw1->opcode decode table AND binding the timing row and override flag into InstrDesc, 701336; plus binding SIX per-instruction strcmps in instr_time_pre_n and decodef to a nmBits bitfield, 696912. Three builds spread across 697k-701k is ONE NUMBER, not a trend -- the +0.6% in the middle was given back by the next measurement, so nothing here should be banked as a gain. The profile attributed 3.19-3.61% to __strcmp_sse42 and 2.05% to instr_decode and removing both changed nothing, because that work filled slots the CPU was already idling through. CONTRAST #166: gating attend()'s clock read bought 105%, because a clock_gettime under the hpet clocksource is a SYSCALL to a memory-mapped timer -- a serialised stall that out-of-order execution cannot hide. THE CHANGES WERE KEPT ANYWAY, on the user's decision and for SIMPLIFICATION rather than speed: the decode table replaced a 256-bucket partition plus a candidate scan, cutting instr_decode to two lines and static footprint from 131.5 KB to 64 KB, and it is proved against the full scan for all 65536 halfwords (YAGPC_DECODE_SELFTEST: 0 mismatches).
  - files `src/cpu_instr.c,src/timing.c,src/instr.h` &middot; symptom `core utilization,optimization no effect,cache misses,IPC 0.82,stalled frontend`
- **#170** *(method)* WHERE REAL TIME RUNS OUT ON THE 2011 i7-3930K, and what costs what: ONE computer holds OPS 201 with about 20% of a core to spare; TWO is where it tips over. Going to OPS 201 costs a lone computer ~64% more than idling in OPS 0; forming a real set costs a further ~26% per computer on top. BOTH matter and the OPS is the larger -- do not attribute the whole cost to the sync machinery.
  - evidence: Measured 2026-09-17 after the day's optimisation work (340906 -> 730568 steps/s, 2.14x). The test is the THREAD STATE, which needs no instrumentation: a pacer that is keeping up SLEEPS (hrtimer_nanosleep) because it is ahead of the wall clock, while one that cannot is pinned in state R with wchan 0. 1 GPC / 1 CRT / OPS 0: 48% of a core, sleeping, keeps up. 1 GPC / 1 CRT / OPS 201: 78.8%, keeps up with less margin. 2 GPC / 2 CRT / OPS 201: 99% + 99%, both pinned, does not keep up -- but runs CLEAN (zero fail-votes, fail-to-sync, BCE receive timeouts, error terminations; both displays driven, zero transfers abandoned). 4 GPC / 2 CRT / OPS 201: 4 x 99%, loses about 5 minutes of simulated time over a run. THE FIRST COMPARISON MADE WAS CONFOUNDED and is corrected here: 1 GPC in OPS 0 against 2 GPCs in OPS 201 changes the computer count AND the OPS together, so it cannot say which caused the extra cost; the user caught it. The 1-GPC OPS 201 run separates them, and OPS 201 IS REACHED -- mass memory goes busy four times after the request, which is the computer loading OPS 2 overlays. A single computer can hold OPS 201 because set membership is DATA: DM6OPS takes the target set from the GPC Requirements Table indexed by the OPS requested, and a GRT entry naming only itself never syncs with anybody. Script: examples/.1gpc-ops201.script (diagnostic, hidden).
  - config `G2` &middot; symptom `real time,does not keep up,core utilization,OPS 201`
- **#173** *(emulator)* A vehicle restored from a snapshot is NOT a redundant set: the computers vote against each other, and once the votes clear they no longer transition in common -- OPS xxx PRO has to be entered separately on each GPC instead of one carrying the other. Restoring per-machine state is not sufficient to restore a SET.
  - evidence: A RESTORED VEHICLE LEFT ALONE IS CLEAN. 2-GPC 2-CRT OPS 201 restored from a snapshot and held ten minutes with no input at all: 0 votes, 0 fail-to-sync, 0 BCE receive timeouts, 0 error terminations, 0 barrier abandonments, 0 display peer holds, 0 panel-silent holds. Repeated with RUN -> STBY -> RUN played into GPC1: equally clean, which clears #175 as the cause. Both ran at roughly 97-99% of real time once the harness's own start-up delays are accounted for -- against the ~80% an interactive session showed, the difference being the manager, the GUI and a person driving it. So the earlier reading of this entry -- that a restored vehicle diverges of its own accord -- is NOT supported: it does not. WHAT THE CLEAN RUNS DID NOT TEST is the path the voting session actually used. They start simulatePASS fresh with --snapshot-resume, which builds a vehicle in a new process. The manager's Restore button does something else entirely: Launcher.stop(keep={'manager'}) and bring_up() INSIDE a running process, stopping six children, keeping one, and relaunching into the same port base, the same multicast groups and the same discrete buses. Every clean run has taken the easy path. That is now the hypothesis and the next experiment -- restore, then restore again in place through the session port, and watch the CAM across the second one. Earlier evidence on this entry (votes with zero wall-clock holds, so not slowness) stands and is what makes it worth chasing.
  - procs `CPU,ICC` &middot; config `G3` &middot; env `--snapshot,--resume,YAGPC_BARRIER_US` &middot; files `src/vehicle.c,src/vehicle.h,src/iccmodel.c,src/run.c,discretePanel/simulatePASS.py` &middot; symptom `votes against after restore,OPS transition not common,each GPC needs its own OPS PRO,restored set not redundant`
- **#174** *(emulator)* A restored display shows FOREGROUND DATA ONLY: no background fixed format text, and the foreground periodically blanks or shows wrong data before correcting itself. The DEU/IDP picture is not part of a snapshot, so PASS's periodic field updates arrive against a display that was never loaded with the format they belong to.
  - evidence: Observed 2026-09-18 on a 2-GPC OPS 201/301 restore, and it is the expected consequence rather than a defect in the capture: --snapshot writes per-machine CPU/IOP state and main storage, and nothing else. The static format text arrives once, at DEU LOAD time, as a FORMAT FILL -- measured at 8 for a whole 420 s run (README.md:262-266), so it is an EVENT and not a periodic refresh; a capture taken afterwards has no record of it and a restore never replays it. The changing fields are written continuously (367 displayFills and 569 timeFills in the same run), which is why the foreground appears at all. This is the gap the rewritten plan calls Stage 2, and this observation is its Stage 0 input: what came back is the foreground, what did not is the format. Cheapest candidate fix first: DEUUnit.requestLoad (MEDS2.py:1826) per IDP, letting PASS repaint; if that does not restore the whole format, save DEUUnit state and replay it via _sendToMDUs in 509-halfword chunks as _clearMDUs (MEDS2.py:10305) already does with zeros.
  - procs `DK6,DK7,DK8` &middot; env `--snapshot,--resume` &middot; files `discretePanel/MEDS2.py,discretePanel/simulatePASS.py,src/deumodel.c` &middot; symptom `foreground only after restore,no background fixed data,format text missing,foreground blanks then corrects`
- **#176** *(emulator)* A RESUMED MACHINE NEVER ANNOUNCES ITS OUTPUT DISCRETES, so nothing ever learns them. discretes_publish_out sends only the bits that CHANGED and returns early when none did; a resumed machine loads regDiscreteOut out of its snapshot, so by the time the software next touches it nothing has changed and the register is never put on the wire -- while every listener starts from nothing. The crew panel's gpc_out is 'None until heard', so its MODE talkback sits at barberpole for the whole run, and the peers, wired through outHook, never learn that computer's lines either.
  - evidence: Found by a crew script that waited for the talkback before doing anything else: 'WAIT TIMED OUT after 300 s: wait gpc 2 mode-tb RUN timeout 300'. The vehicle was healthy by every other measure -- it ran 2 h 16 min, 6,139,962,517 instructions on GPC1 and 6,171,541,426 on GPC2, 8196.5 s of simulated time, 4270 display fills to MEDS1, the barrier held 191,955,595 times with 0 abandoned -- and its MODE talkback never once read RUN. discretes.c: 'uint32_t changed = before ^ after; ... if (changed == 0) return;' and the outHook call sits after that return. THIS IS THE SAME PUBLISH-ON-CHANGE TRAP the restore invariants already name for cam.py, which only rebuilds its matrix because a fresh yaGPC2 has value[] zeroed and so its first publish IS a change -- a resumed machine is exactly the case where that reasoning fails, because the value it comes up holding is the one it means to keep. Note also that even when something later does change a bit, only the delta goes out, so a listener that never heard the base value can never reconstruct it. FIX: on a --resume, publish the restored register with ~value as the previous value, so every bit counts as changed and the whole word goes out as one SET and one RESET. Whether this also accounts for #173's votes is NOT established -- 2 h 16 min in this state produced no votes at all.
  - procs `CPU` &middot; env `--resume,--snapshot` &middot; files `src/run.c,src/discretes.c,discretePanel/panelO6.py` &middot; symptom `MODE talkback stays barberpole after a restore,wait gpc mode-tb RUN times out,talkback never RUN,peers never learn a restored computer's lines`
- **#180** *(emulator)* RESTORE-TIME MUTUAL VOTES ARE A LOST I/O AT THE CAPTURE INSTANT, AND WHETHER THEY APPEAR DEPENDS ON WHETHER BOTH COMPUTERS LOSE IT. Right after a resume both machines put up 010 IPR (input problem report) together and carry on -- that run is CLEAN; when only one does, each declares the other failed to sync within 10 ms (FCMISYNC on one, FCMTSYNC on the other, redundant-set failures, landmark 1948c r0=00080001/00100001). The lost input is a transfer that was in flight when the capture was taken: the display and peripheral PROCESSES restart fresh on a restore, so anything mid-transfer on their side is gone even though the GPC's own BCE state is restored faithfully. Measured rates with one binary: the owner's snapshot201 CLEAN 5/5 -- and it is the ONLY capture where GPC2 commands no display bus (taken while GPC1 drove both CRTs, the #179 defect); every capture with two display-bus commanders is flaky, 1 of 3 to 0 of 6, and the SAME capture can go either way run to run. RULED OUT: #179's stale sync copy; the ICC, mass memory and timing unit being uncaptured (captured now, and both live ICC queues were EMPTY in every capture); restoring the barrier spread, which made it worse (9 of 9 voting with it on) and is off behind YAGPC_BARRIER_RESTORE=1; settle time before the capture (10 minutes at OPS 201 is still 1 of 3); the BCE latch state, which does not separate clean from voting captures. NEXT: quiesce the display/peripheral transfers before the rendezvous, or refuse a capture taken mid-transfer -- 'refuse, don't degrade', as the plan says of the peer hold.  CAVEAT ADDED AFTER #187: every rate above was scored by syncscore.py from a cam.log WITH NO TIMESTAMPS, and a run's SIGINT teardown casts votes of its own.  A run counted here as voting may have been voting only at the shutdown, so THESE RATES ARE AN UPPER BOUND on restore-time sync loss and the clean/voting split may be softer than it looks.  cam.py stamps its log now and syncscore.py separates the teardown votes; re-score before building on the numbers.
  - evidence: Restore rates, one 2-min hold each, port base 7300, nested X display, synctest.sh + syncscore.py, all on builds with #179's fix. The owner's snapshot201 (taken 09-18, BEFORE that fix): CLEAN 5 of 5. Every capture taken after it: snap3 4 of 6 / 1 of 1 / 1 of 3; snap4 0 of 12; snap5 (ten minutes' settling) 1 of 3; snap6 (set-agreement check) 4 of 9; snap7 (stricter mid-receive check, never fired) 1 of 9; snap8 (ONE CRT, so a single display-bus commander, the one structural feature snapshot201 has and the others lack) 0 of 3. REFUTED: the one-commander configuration; settle time; the barrier spread (9 of 9 voting with it on, off by default behind YAGPC_BARRIER_RESTORE=1); the BCE latch state, which does not separate clean from voting captures; and 'the clean ones are not a redundant set' -- the reverse holds, a CLEAN restore shows 28883 I/O-complete syncs against 4 in a voting one, so the protected I/O stops because the set broke. CONFIRMED MECHANISM: both machines raise 010 IPR together right after a resume and carry on (clean); when only one does, FCMISYNC on one and FCMTSYNC on the other each fail the other within 10 ms (landmark 1948c, r0=00080001/00100001, redundant-set failures). WHAT SNAPSHOT201 HAS THAT THE OTHERS DO NOT IS STILL UNKNOWN.
  - addrs `1948c` &middot; csects `FCMSFAIL,FCMISYNC,FCMTSYNC` &middot; env `YAGPC_BARRIER_RESTORE,YAGPC_SYNCTRACE,YAGPC_LANDMARKS` &middot; files `src/vehicle.c,src/iccmodel.c,src/mmumodel.c` &middot; symptom `restored vehicle votes,mutual fail votes after restore,snapshot fragile,restore not a redundant set` &middot; run `snap3-a,snap3-b,snap3-c,snap4-a,snap4-b,snap4-c,s201-now1..5`
- **#182** *(iop)* A BCE WHOSE MIA TRANSMITTER IS DISABLED STILL PUTS DATA WORDS ON THE BUS. exec_CMD gates the command word on regXmitEna, but exec_TDS, exec_TDLI, exec_MOUT and exec_MOUT_at queue their DMA reads ungated, and iop.c's DMA service calls mia_xmit_word with no check -- so only the command is withheld and the data streams out anyway. The BCE Principles of Operation (IBM-6246556A part 3) says the opposite twice: #MOUT 'if it finds either its transmitter disabled or its MIA busy at a time when it should be idle, the BCE will terminate the #MOUT, set its Program Exception Bit to 0 ... and enter the Wait State'; and 'A BCE in Listen Mode ... will not transmit either commands or data. The instructions #CMDI, #CMD, #MOUT, #TDS, #TDLI, #TDL ... [are] error terminat[ed] ... A BCE in Listen Mode should never be told to transmit data.' SEEN AS: after GPC/CRT 2 2 EXEC in OPS 0, GPC1 drops BCE7 and GPC2 never enables it, yet CRT2 keeps receiving 258-halfword fills and shows GPC2's content. DO NOT GATE THE DATA WITHOUT FIRST FINDING WHY THE NEW COMMANDER NEVER ENABLES ITS TRANSMITTER: gating alone would leave CRT2 with no commander at all, which is POLL FAIL -- what the owner saw after the same entry in OPS 201.
  - evidence: YAGPC_XMITENA_TRACE, run gpccrt: t=42.0 GPC1 holds buses 1,6,7,8,10-24; t=43.3 GPC2 holds 2,18,19,24 only; t=103.3 (the GPC/CRT entry) GPC1 drops bus 7; no GPC enables it afterwards. meds2.log shows IDP2 still taking fills to the end of the run, and panel.log shows CRT2 reading '0001/ / GPC MEMORY 2'. Only one code path writes regXmitEna (iop.c:2264, the MIA TRANSMITTER ENABLE/DISABLE PCO), so the trace is complete.
  - csects `FCMBMASK,FCMBUSCM` &middot; instrs `#MOUT,#TDS,#TDLI,#CMD` &middot; procs `BCE6,BCE7` &middot; env `YAGPC_XMITENA_TRACE` &middot; files `src/iop_bce_instr.c,src/iop.c` &middot; symptom `listener transmits,dual commanders,POLL FAIL after GPC/CRT,display driven by a GPC with its transmitter disabled` &middot; doc `IBM-6246556A part 3, BCE PoO` &middot; run `gpccrt`
- **#187** *(method)* FAIL VOTES IN THE LAST SECONDS OF A RUN ARE THE SHUTDOWN, NOT A FAILURE.  simulatePASS stops the emulator with SIGINT and the machine threads die one at a time, so the survivors correctly vote the first one out and cam.py ends with lit lamps -- '44 34 14 24 ON' in a 4-GPC run, '21 12 ON' in a 2-GPC one.  Nothing is wrong with the vehicle.  Before this was understood, a clean run read as a failed one because cam.py's log carried NO TIME and the last four lines looked identical to a mid-run collapse.  cam.py now stamps every line ('voting: 12  ON (bus)  [t=330.5]', seconds since cam start, appended at the END so syncscore.py's voting:\\s+(\\d)(\\d) still matches).  RULE: read the stamp before treating a lit lamp as evidence -- compare it with the last CMD shared= in yaGPC2.log; if they agree within a second or two it is teardown.
  - evidence: Run 4gpc3 (4 GPCs, 2 CRTs, corrected script, stamped cam.py): votes 44/34/14/24 all at cam t=668.1, and the last command words from the machines still running are at shared t=668.794 (bus 6 gpc1, bus 7 gpc2) -- 0.7 s apart on two independent clocks, at the instant the run stops.  No lamp lit at any earlier point in a 700 s run.  Run 4gpc2, same script unstamped, ends the same way with the same four votes; run fixed (2 GPCs) ends with 21 and 12 while both machines commanded their buses to t=775.3 of a 780 s run.
  - procs `CPU` &middot; env `YAGPC_CMDTRACE` &middot; files `discretePanel/cam.py` &middot; symptom `fail vote at end of run,cam lamps lit at shutdown,GPC4 voted out,mutual fail vote 21 12,voted out at teardown` &middot; run `4gpc3,4gpc2,fixed`
- **#188** *(method)* A SCRIPT WITH 'wait user' CANNOT BE RUN UNATTENDED, AND FAILS SILENTLY WHEN IT IS.  examples/4gpc-startup-subtitled.script begins with 'wait user', which holds until someone CLICKS IN THE PANEL O6 WINDOW (crewscript.py Player._run, kind wait_user).  Started from a script-driven test with no one there, the whole run sits in HALT for its full duration and produces a log with four 'MODE: HALT' lines, no IPL, no commands and NO ERROR -- it looks like a vehicle that refused to start rather than a script waiting for a mouse.  The only notice is one line on simulatePASS's console, 'note: the script has a wait user', which goes to the console and not to any log that gets scored.  To run such a script unattended, copy it without that line: grep -v '^wait user$' examples/NAME.script > examples/.NAME-nowait.script.  THE COPY MUST LIVE IN examples/ -- nested 'script ...' lines resolve relative to the PARENT SCRIPT, so a copy in a scratch directory cannot find ipl-one-gpc.script.
  - evidence: Run 4gpc (4 GPCs, 2 CRTs, 620 s): all four GPCs held 'MODE: HALT; CPU held in reset' from start to finish, yaGPC2.log 4 KB with 0 command words, keyboard1.log and keyboard2.log 0 bytes, cam matrix all zeros, exit code 0.  The same script through a .4gpc-nowait.script copy (runs 4gpc2 and 4gpc3) IPLs all four and reaches OPS 201.
  - files `discretePanel/examples/4gpc-startup-subtitled.script,discretePanel/crewscript.py,discretePanel/simulatePASS.py` &middot; symptom `all GPCs stay in HALT,script does nothing,no IPL,empty keyboard log,run produces no commands` &middot; run `4gpc,4gpc2,4gpc3`
- **#189** *(flight-sw)* THE NBAT ENTRIES ARE NOT NO-OPS, BUT THE FIRST FEW LOOK LIKE IT.  Watching the databus setup it appears that nothing changes and that GPC2's buses are configured exactly as GPC1's already were.  What actually happens: the table is EMPTY before any entry (CONFIG 0(0), every bus item 0), and 'ITEM 1 + 2 EXEC' -- selecting memory configuration 2 -- FILLS IT FROM THE GRT.  The GRT's MC 2 target set is C000, GPCs {1,2} and no others (#92), so the defaults alternate the odd buses to GPC1 and the even ones to GPC2: STRING 1=1 2=2 3=1 4=2, CRT 1=1 2=2, MM 1=1 2=2.  The next keystrokes retype exactly that, which is why the display does not move.  THE ENTRIES THAT DO WORK ARE THE LATER ONES: ITEM 4 + 3 and ITEM 5 + 4 add GPC3 and GPC4 to a target set that defaults to two computers, and ITEM 9 + 3, ITEM 10 + 4 and ITEM 11 + 1 move STRING 3, STRING 4 and PL 1/2 off their two-GPC defaults.  Keep the whole block: on a re-IPL of a set whose buses have drifted, the entries that are redundant here are the ones doing the work.  AND THE SECOND HALF OF THE CONFUSION: an entry typed on CRT2 changes the NBAT on BOTH displays.  Crew keyboard input to a redundant set is a COMMON-SET INPUT -- every GPC in the set processes it and updates its own copy -- so GPC MEMORY 1 and GPC MEMORY 2 move together, CRT1 trailing CRT2 by about a second.  Looking back at CRT1 after typing on CRT2 finds it already agreeing, which reads as 'nothing changed' when in fact both changed.
  - evidence: Run nbat (4 GPCs, 2 CRTs, NSTS_ANNOUNCE_ROWS=all, every frame captured off the screen-announce port base+91, 637 frames).  CRT2, GPC MEMORY 2: t=372.9 '1 CONFIG 0( 0)', GPC slots blank, STRING/CRT/MM all 0.  t=384.4 after ITEM 1 + 2: '1 CONFIG 2(G2)', 'GPC 1 2 _ 0 0', STRING 1=1 2=2 3=1 4=2, CRT 1=1 2=2, MM 1=1 2=2 -- 22 lines changed in one keystroke.  EACH ENTRY LANDS ON ITS OWN FRAME, about 1.9 s apart, with no lag and no batching: t=402.1 'GPC 1 2 3 0 0' (ITEM 4 + 3), t=408.4 'GPC 1 2 3 4 0' (ITEM 5 + 4), t=426.1 STRING 3 1->3 (ITEM 9 + 3), t=433.3 STRING 4 2->4 (ITEM 10 + 4), t=440.1 PL 1/2 0->1 (ITEM 11 + 1).  A first pass here reported the last three as one batched change at t=440.1; that was a FILTER ARTEFACT -- the continuation rows render as '3 9 1 ...' and '4 10 2 ...' with no keyword in them, so a dedupe keyed on CONFIG|STRING|GPC|CRT|PL|MM could not see them change.  Read these frames unfiltered.  Final table before the OPS transition: CONFIG 2(G2), GPC 1 2 3 4 0, STRING 1=1 2=2 3=3 4=4, PL 1/2=1.  NOTE FOR ANYONE REPEATING THIS: the scratchpad is NOT in the announced frame (MEDS draws it from its own geometry), so keystrokes cannot be located that way -- find them by the table changing.  Same five changes on CRT1, 0.5-1.5 s later: 403.6, 409.3, 426.6, 434.3, 440.0 against CRT2's 402.1, 408.4, 426.1, 433.3, 440.1.
  - csects `CZ2COMMO,DM6OPS` &middot; procs `DK2` &middot; env `NSTS_ANNOUNCE_ROWS` &middot; files `discretePanel/MEDS2.py,discretePanel/examples/4gpc-startup-subtitled.script` &middot; symptom `NBAT does nothing,ITEM entries change nothing,GPC2 buses same as GPC1,databus setup no-op,MEM/BUS CONFIG unchanged` &middot; tsec `372-440` &middot; doc `TD115 Rev B sec 5 pp.5-3..5-4; GRT in CZ2COMMO.hal:434` &middot; run `nbat`

## Fixed

- **#14** *(emulator)* MSC repeat state (mscRepeatActive/PC/UntilUs) survived a CPU-forced PC load, so a returning @RAW would not re-arm and used a stale deadline.
  - evidence: Correct as modelling; cleared on the local-store PC write. Did NOT unblock phase 18.
  - addrs `a201-a201` &middot; csects `FIOMDLY` &middot; instrs `@RAW` &middot; procs `MSC` &middot; files `src/iop.c` &middot; run `v55a`
- **#15** *(emulator)* exec_RDL read its count with a HALFWORD access masked to 16 bits and did not ignore the address LSB.
  - evidence: BCE POO: count is bits 14-31 of the main storage FULLWORD, LSB of the address ignored, range 0..262143. Same defect already fixed in its twin #TDL. LATENT: #RDL never executes here (BCE18 uses #RDLI).
  - instrs `#RDL,#RDLI,#TDL` &middot; procs `BCE18` &middot; files `src/iop_bce_instr.c` &middot; doc `BCE PoO IBM-6246556A pt3 (count = bits 14-31 of the fullword)` &middot; run `v84a`
- **#32** *(method)* gpc-buslog.py's KIND table had both enum pairs transposed. Correct order is XMIT_WORD=0, XMIT_CMD=1, RECV_WORD=2, RECV_POLL=3 (yaGpcIntegration.h:177) -- word before command, the opposite of the way one says it.
  - evidence: The wrong table made an ordinary DEU fill (1 command + 100 data words, emitted at a silence boundary) decode as '100 commands issued in zero simulated time', which reads like a bus program spinning. Cross-check that settles it: bus 6 XMIT_WORD must equal the DEU model's own wordsIn -- 111164/111164 for bus 6, 60957 for bus 7, 61477 for bus 8, all exact. Analyses that selected kind==1 for MM commands were unaffected (that IS XMIT_CMD) and are independently corroborated by the MMU model's log.
  - env `YAGPC_BUSLOG` &middot; files `gpc-buslog.py` &middot; doc `yaGpcIntegration.h:177 GpcServiceNumber` &middot; run `v91a`
- **#58** *(tape)* SECOND build defect in the same root load block: 0x1e8, 0x202, 0x218 and 0x22c hold REAL but WRONG Z-CON values, not fill. Tape/runtime have 117c, 13ec, 0be8, 0cc0; all 8 DASS dumps have 88be, 89f6, 85f4, 8660. Every DASS value has bit 0 SET (so a sector is applied) and every one of ours has it CLEAR (so the target is taken as sector 0). Deliberately NOT patched by tools/patch_root_zcons.py -- these want their own diagnosis rather than a hand-applied constant.
  - evidence: DIAGNOSED 2026-09-11 (#75): the four values are 2x the DASS value mod 2^16 (117c = 2*88be, 13ec = 2*89f6, 0be8 = 2*85f4, 0cc0 = 2*8660) -- every relocation to #CDSPSPC/#CDPDSPC/#CDXCCCS/#CDXRDMM was applied twice because each module was linked twice. The 'bit 0 clear' was the carry out of the doubling, not a missing sector. v42: all four equal SSW and G9; root block 0x1a8-0x247 160/160. ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: Found by comparing the whole 160-halfword load block at dest=0x1a8 against DASS G9.fcm: 40 of 160 halfwords differed, 36 of them the 18 fill Z-CONs, leaving exactly these 4. The #ZDCDDS4/ACOS collision was in the whole-memory G9 link only.
  - addrs `1e8,202,218,22c,1e2` &middot; csects `#ZDCDDS4,ACOS` &middot; tape `v36,v41,v42` &middot; symptom `zcon high bit clear,sector 0 target`
- **#64** *(method)* YAGPC_BUSLOG LOST ITS TAIL on every SIGINT-terminated run -- which is how every HEALTHY run ends -- because its 1 MB stdio buffer (~87,000 events) was never written. Worse, when bus traffic collapses (the thing the log exists to catch) the whole collapse fits in that buffer and vanishes, so the file simply stops before the answer. Fixed: fflush once per simulated second.
  - evidence: b901.bus ended at 292.0 s and b201.bus at 273.4 s of 404 s runs, both at ~32 MB. With normal DEU polling at ~1,500 events/s the missing 110-130 s would have forced several flushes, so the truncation itself implied a collapse -- but hid when.
  - env `YAGPC_BUSLOG` &middot; files `src/run.c` &middot; run `b901,b201`
- **#67** *(method)* YAGPC_RINGTRIG could not see FULLWORD stores: cpu_store_fw passed the 32-bit value to a 16-bit comparison, so STM, ST and every PSW save were invisible and the trigger's silence read as 'nothing ever wrote this'. Fixed: a fullword store is tested as two halfword stores at addr and addr+1.
  - evidence: W901 watched FP's R7 slot (932e) for acf8, the value STM would put there, and never fired -- through a run whose ring shows FPMGMTIM's BR 7 landing on 0x1acf8 eight times.
  - env `YAGPC_RINGTRIG` &middot; files `src/cpu.c` &middot; run `W901`
- **#73** *(goal)* POLL FAIL after OPS 201, 301, 801 and 901 is FIXED on OI340700-v41boot.mmv, produced from v36 by tools/patch_unresolved.py (88 halfwords in 7 load blocks, byte-identical to v41, idempotent). The cause throughout is the build's UNRESOLVED CROSS-PHASE RELOCATIONS; the real repair is in the per-phase links (see #59, #60), and the tool is a labelled workaround until then.
  - evidence: Headless, bus-log measured: OPS 201 steady to end of run on V201 (v39) and Z201 (v41), B201/B201b (v38+poison); OPS 301 steady on V301 (v39), Z301 (v41), vs K301 (v37) silent after ~285 s; OPS 901 steady on Y901a and Y901b (v41) with the G9 MDM buses 10/11 carrying traffic for the first time, vs X901b (v40) silent and X901a (v40) crashing at 0x06a8d. INTERACTIVE, by the user with MEDS on v41: OPS 201 (UNIV PTG), 301 (DEORB MNVR COAST, title now shown), 302 (DEORB MNVR EXEC), 801 (FCS/DED DIS C/O), 901 and many GNC 9 displays, 101 (LAUNCH TRAJ 1). Tool check: v36 -> tool -> byte-identical to v41; a second run fills nothing, 0 refused, exit 0. An intermediate version with wildcarded neighbours but no distinctiveness guard wrote a Z-CON value into fill inside an UNRELATED load block (flat 1186050) -- the MIN_ANCHOR guard exists because of that.
  - addrs `1d6-243,1a7ef,1b63e,1bbf7,43c78,43c84,11f0,1ebaa-1ee25,1de62-1df0b` &middot; csects `FIOPDSMU,FIOG9ADB,FIOPDG9,FIOSVCP,FIOCMPLT,FIOPDISP,$0DGRGSE,#DDGRGSE` &middot; tape `v37,v38,v39,v40,v41` &middot; files `tools/patch_unresolved.py,tools/patch_unresolved.fills.json` &middot; symptom `POLL FAIL` &middot; run `D201,B201,B201b,V201,Z201,K301,V301,Z301,X901a,X901b,Y901a,Y901b`
- **#74** *(tape)* The OI340700 v41 volume is now REPRODUCIBLE FROM SOURCE by yaGPC2/tapebuild/build.sh, as documented in HANDOFF-OPS9.md sections 0-3: run from an empty directory on 2026-09-10 it produced OI340700-v41boot.mmv BYTE-IDENTICAL to the tested volume (and its pre-fill OI340700-v36boot.mmv byte-identical to v36).
  - evidence: Before: the handoff described the v27 procedure; v36 had been built by /tmp/claude-1000/buildtape.sh (never committed); a rebuild from ~/pass-build differed in 118,176 halfwords because objects/AIGDEU.obj was left from the v79 WAIT experiment. Found and fixed on the way, each verified by comparison: (1) compilePASS needs --sdl (160 PROGRAM objects otherwise gain START/stack ERs) and --release=OI340700 (CPUSLS/CPTOSV CARDTYPE ACBC; else 8 phase-15 objects cascade away) -- then 1,981/1,981 objects match in ESD/TXT/RLD; (2) dfg was Don's UNCOMMITTED working tree: pinned as upstream 7d90b05 + toolchain-patches/dfg-7d90b05-to-OI340700.patch, verified file-for-file; (3) compilePASS's dfg --release probe never matched because rich styles the two dashes separately -- fixed; without it CS2120 VPD is 00D3 where the S2 DASS dump has 00D5; (4) lib/runtime/RUN is RUNASM with --fill=C6C6 (205/205 byte-identical), ZCON from compilePASS; (5) SYSLIBL1 = one hard link per SD, name order, first definer wins, START excluded (4,275/4,275); (6) sdfpad = 3360-byte SDFs + one zero byte; pchsrc = SSSRC/PCH*.asm without extension; (7) per-phase csect tables regenerate byte-identically from PFS/mafgen; extsyms-02-plus.json (generator lost) committed; (8) DEU load modules = 24 blocks extracted from pass-910.mmv into inputs/deu-loadmodules.mmv; (9) CSPCLB source fixes committed as a patch; (10) toolchain = fork db9d34b + 3 branches, tree a68da6e6 verified; (11) con80build links NOTHING unless build/lib/runtime/{RUN,ZCON} exist in the toolchain checkout -- found only by following the document from an empty directory; now pointed at our own runtime. The build is deterministic (two identical builds byte-identical).
  - csects `AIGDEU,CS2120,CPUSLS,CPTOSV` &middot; tape `v36,v41` &middot; files `tapebuild/build.sh,tapebuild/derive.py,tapebuild/link-and-cut.sh,PASS.REL32V0/compilePASS,HANDOFF-OPS9.md` &middot; symptom `not reproducible`
- **#75** *(tape)* One OBJECT linked TWICE in phase 2 applies every relocation in it twice. DSPSPC.obj (worklist) has #DDSPSPC/#XDSPSPC deferred to a later phase by dropDeferredSections; the reference then pulls the minimal library's #CDSPSPC.obj -- a hard link to the SAME object -- so #CDSPSPC and #ZDSPSPC exist twice, both pinned to the same address, and BOTH RLD sets are applied to the one image. - **#85** *(emulator)* With MEDS2.py (or any display unit in another process) the GPCIPL page is hit-and-miss: the clock counts over a blank page. Cause: the DK peer answers LATE IN WALL TIME. MEDS2.py's IDP shares one Python event loop with the MDU's drawing and sometimes cannot read the socket for tens of ms; GPCIPL gives the DK BCE a 5.0 ms message time out (MTO 303) and the MSC gives up sooner, so the BCE error-terminates (ERROR 44 BCE TIME-OUT N RETRY FAIL-DEUIPL, 96 REAL TIME MSC TIME OUT, 97, 99). GPCIPL re-IPLs the unit (a second zero-fill load) and after a second failure gives up on it, never sending the one-shot 509+254-halfword menu fill; time fills still flow, hence the clock. Fixed in yaGPC2: a peer in another process may HOLD the machine (no simulated time passes) while a reply it owes is on its way -- iop.c peerWait, bcenet_framer_peer_wait -- so the reply lands inside the software's own window.
  - evidence: Reproduced in private network namespaces (unshare -rn, Xephyr, Mesa EGL): v44 tape, two MEDS2.py instances, scripted discretePanel. Without the hold 4 of 4 runs failed: DEU zero-filled 2-3 times, menu never sent, 46-60 BCE6 RECV TIMEOUTs (waited=5.00 ms), SVCs 44/96/97/99. With it 7 of 7 showed the menu (screenshot: GPCIPL MENU (1) ... GPCIPL 09.05.00.00.01 LOADED): one load, 0-1 BCE6 time outs, ~150 holds per run, longest 57-65 ms wall. Full run to PASS (ITEM 1 EXEC on KYBD1, RUN): GPC MEMORY drawn by MEDS2.py, 378 holds, 0 time outs. User's logs: 0911-0821 one load then menu; 0911-0904 two loads. Lengthening the time out instead was already refuted (iop.c RECV_TIMEOUT_FLOOR_US: 20 ms floor = load never completes). Headless --deu-model unaffected (no hook installed): DEU image byte-identical; unit tests identical counts to HEAD.
