- **#88** *(emulator)* The MEDS header clock -- the one on the GPCIPL menu screens -- always started near 000/05:47 and never showed the real time of day. TWO defects in series. (a) The MTU model put its three time halfwords at wire offset 2, behind two zero header words, so FPMLIMCK read 0x0000 as GMT days, failed CHI R2,GE,X'0001' for all three accumulators, and took FPMMTURM's default LHI R5,X'0030' DEFAULT GMT TO 1 DAY = 86400 s. (b) MEDS2.py's IDP-to-MDU MDUMsg.CLOCK carries HALFWORDS, so 86403 wrapped to 20867 s = 000/05:47:47. (c) and separately, the MTU reported ELAPSED simulated time from day 000 rather than a time of day, so even an accepted reading would have been wrong.
  - evidence: The MTU's layout is stated four independent ways, all agreeing that the six halfwords are GMT at 0,1,2 and MET at 3,4,5, from the base: FIOPRMPG's commander does #LBR TFCMMTU1 then #MIN 0,6 (buffer register AT the base); FCMCBLKS' FPMMIBAT is DC Y(0),Y(TFCMMTU1),Y(TFCMMTU2),Y(TFCMMTU3) -- buffer STARTS; DCD14201.hal takes %COPY(DL(19),TFCMMTU1$(1:), 6); and FPMMTURM's FPMLIMCK reads 0(R0) and 1(R0) for GMT, 3(R0) and 4(R0) for MET, with AHI R0,3 POINT TO MET TIME before converting. The LA R3,TFCMMTU1+2 that commit b4a7a6cf6 relied on is in the PCMMU branch (FPMMTURM lines 265-365), where the PMU's 5 Hz voted GMT is read into the MTU buffers and +2 is the MILLISECONDS word, sampled to detect the PCMMU clock ticking. Field packing confirmed independently: FPMLIMCK's seconds limit X'0164' is exactly BCD 0x59 shifted up by the two spare low bits the model already left, and LH on this machine appends 16 low-order zeros (instruction set sec 4.18), which is what makes FPMMTUFX's SLDL 2/4/4/2/4 digit peeling and FPMLIMCK's SRL 6 / NHI X'003F' agree on one layout. MET left zero: FPMLIMCK's MET tests have no lower bound. Measured: PASS commands the MTU at t=223.7 s (the OPS transition, NOT at IPL -- a run must reach it, so a 300 s wall-clock headless run ends just short), and the DEU time fills go from mission=99.5 (000/00:01:40, elapsed since boot) to mission=22010899.040 = 254/18:08:19, the host's own date and local time.
  - csects `FPMMTURM,FPMLIMCK,FPMMTUFX,FIOPRMPG,FPMMIBAT,TFCMMTU1,DCD14201,DMIMCD` &middot; procs `BCE20,BCE21,BCE22,DK6` &middot; tape `pass-ipl-cflm` &middot; env `YAGPC_TIMEFILL,YAGPC_MTUTRACE,--date-time-epoch` &middot; files `src/mtumodel.c,src/run.c,src/deumodel.c,yaShuttle/discretePanel/MEDS2.py` &middot; symptom `menu clock starts 00:05:30,clock never shows time of day,mission=86403,000/05:47:47,GMT defaults to 1 day` &middot; tsec `223.7` &middot; doc `AP-101S instruction set sec 4.18 LOAD HALFWORD; sec 6.3 SHIFT LEFT DOUBLE LOGICAL` &middot; run `mtu-g,mtu-i`
- **#89** *(emulator)* The simulated clock ran at about 2/3 of real time (13 min of MEDS header clock per 20 min of wall), and a mass-memory load that took 45 s on the first ITEM 1 EXEC took 68 s on every re-IPL after a HALT -- the same factor, 1/0.66. NOT a speed problem: the emulator used 15.7% of ONE core of 20 and was in hrtimer_nanosleep in 92% of stack samples. The defect was in rtpacer's REBASE, which moves the wall/sim origin pair to now and forgets whatever the simulation was behind by. It was being applied on every wake from a wait state, on any deficit past STALL_REBASE_MS while executing, and on a slow bus peer -- none of which are host stalls.
  - evidence: Instrumented (YAGPC_PACETRACE now reports rebases by cause and what the wait loop delivers), the arithmetic closed exactly: 'wall= 169.49s sim= 134.32s rate=0.792 | slept 7.17s | rebase 2807 (35.0s written off); by cause: pace 72/18.0s wake 2735/17.0s'. The 35.2 s the clock was missing and the 35.0 s the rebases wrote off are the same seconds. The same run SLEPT 7 s for being ahead while writing off 35 s for being behind -- it cannot be both, and that contradiction is the tell: leads were surrendered by rtpacer_pace's sleep and deficits destroyed by the rebase, so the error could only move one way. ROOT CAUSE in rtpacer_advance_idle: it advanced simulated time to match wall measured from p->idleStartWallSeconds, which rtpacer_enter_idle re-anchors on EVERY entry into the wait state -- so it tracked 1:1 from wherever it happened to be and faithfully PRESERVED the deficit, which the wake rebase then deleted. PASS is in the wait state 65% of its life (wait loop: 108 s wall of 169 s), which is precisely where the time can be won back: the machine is idle, nothing observable happens, the next event is determined. Measuring against the run's own origin lets a wait fast-forward up to the wall clock and never past it; cpu_advance_idle_ns still stops at the next timer expiry and the instant the wait clears, so no interrupt moves. Result 'wall= 483.62s sim= 483.62s rate=1.000 | rebase 0', wall-minus-sim gap 0.00 s sampled every 60 s across 8 minutes, and a run ending 21:30:45 wall showed 254/21:30:38 on the DEU. TWO TRAPS: (a) skipping the wait loop's sleep whenever behind AT ALL busy-spins on the clock -- 747 million passes in a 170 s run at ~15 ns per clock_gettime, measured; RTPACE_CATCHUP_MS is the threshold worth chasing. (b) Waste is not the same as the defect: batchrunner_step disassembled every instruction to a string, snapshotted the register file twice and diffed it with a strcmp per register, and ran a SECOND instr_decode per instruction (30% of all CPU) just to notice an undecodable one -- gating that and letting cpu_exec1 report decodeFailed moved the rate 0.792 -> 0.854 and none of the rest of the way.
  - csects `rtpacer,batchrunner_step,cpu_advance_idle_ns,rtpacer_advance_idle,rtpacer_rebase` &middot; tape `pass-ipl-cflm` &middot; env `YAGPC_PACETRACE,YAGPC_RANGETRACE` &middot; files `src/rtpacer.c,src/rtpacer.h,src/run.c,src/cpu.c,src/cpu.h` &middot; symptom `clock runs at 2/3 speed,13 minutes per 20,re-IPL slower than first IPL,45 s becomes 68 s,MM load takes longer after HALT` &middot; doc `none -- measured` &middot; run `pace-e,pace-k,pace-m`
- **#90** *(emulator)* With --bce-network (real MEDS2/panelO6 processes on the bus, which is what retest-crt2.sh runs) the emulator could not hold 1:1 with real time even after the rebase fix of #89: the user measured ~6 minutes lost in 38, and yaGPC2 pegged at 102% of a core. The headless in-process configuration was fine at 15% CPU, so the cost was thought to be UDP. The dominant cost was NOT UDP: the IOP was being stepped TWICE for every wait state, because cpu_advance_idle_ns() (cpu.c) carries the clock through a wait and services the IOP itself, but AP101.iopNextPassUs is in ap101.c and was never updated, so the first instruction after the wait replayed every 0.5 us pass of the whole wait.
  - evidence: ap101_step_iop() takes one IOP slice per iop_pass_us() (0.5 us -- BCE PoO 3.4.1's 16.5 us MIA sampling over 33 slices) and records the next in AP101.iopNextPassUs. cpu_advance_idle_ns() advances elapsedTimeUs through the wait and calls iop_exec_idle() every IOP_SLICE_NS (500 ns), but cannot see iopNextPassUs. PASS is in the wait state for 95% of its simulated life, so nearly every pass was a duplicate: 462 million in a 250 s run where 24 million is right, and each BCE sampled its MIA twice as often as the hardware does. ap101_iop_resync() re-anchors the pass clock; the skipped passes are exactly the duplicates because iop_exec_channel_control() is an empty stub, so iop_exec_idle() already does everything iop_exec() does. Effect: exec1 2.007 -> 0.861 us per instruction, CPU 14.4% -> 8.8%, with the SAME final machine state to the bit (FP0-FP7, PSW1/PSW2, NIA=01df8, CC, BSR, DSR) and the same mass-memory milestones. Four further per-instruction costs, each measured: (a) the discretes socket was drained on EVERY READ DISCRETE INPUT PCI and the flight software reads those in tight loops -- ~11M recvfrom() in 170 s, now rate-limited to 250 us of wall; (b) DInstr (688 bytes) was memset TWICE per instruction, once in cpu_exec1 and again as instr_decode's first statement; (c) instr_decode scanned all 135 opcode descriptors, now bucketed by hw1's top 8 bits to 3.5 candidates (YAGPC_DECODE_SELFTEST=1 proves 0 mismatches over all 65536 hw1); (d) the bus sockets were drained with a recvfrom() per bus per service, now one poll() for all of them. A full 65536-entry hw1->descriptor table was tried and is WORSE (0.479 -> 0.691 us): 512 KB indexed randomly per instruction misses cache, where the small sorted array stayed in L1. NET, with DOUBLE the display load (4 MEDS): rate 1.000 sustained, exec1 0.756 us per instruction against 1.07 us of simulated time delivered -- execution now runs at 1.4x real time instead of 0.53x -- and process CPU 15.5% -> ~10%. MEASUREMENT TRAP: stray MEDS2.py processes from killed runs stay joined to the bus and rendering (timeout kills the script, not its children); fourteen of them invalidated a series of A/B comparisons and made the bucketed decode look slower than the linear scan.
  - csects `ap101_step_iop,ap101_iop_resync,cpu_advance_idle_ns,instr_decode,iop_discrete_overlay,bcenet_framer_flush_tick,bcenet_transport_poll_ready` &middot; tape `v44` &middot; env `YAGPC_PACETRACE,YAGPC_DECODE_SELFTEST,YAGPC_BUS_SERVICE_US` &middot; files `src/ap101.c,src/ap101.h,src/cpu.c,src/cpu_instr.c,src/discretes.c,src/run.c,src/bcenet_framer.c,src/bcenet_transport.c` &middot; symptom `loses 6 minutes in 38,102% CPU with bce-network,cannot hold real time with MEDS attached,clock still slow after rebase fix` &middot; doc `AP-101S BCE PoO 3.4.1 (16.5 us MIA sampling)` &middot; run `net-n,net-v`
- **#93** *(emulator)* EVERY run has been IPLing TWICE, single-GPC runs included, since the discrete staleness logic existed. A firmware IPL fills memory and reads seventy-odd blocks off a tape without once polling the discrete socket, so every bit the crew panel was driving aged out against the WALL clock -- not because the panel stopped talking but because the machine stopped listening. The backlog then refreshed them a datagram at a time, and the mode switch is evaluated after each one (it must be: a pushbutton is a pulse, and draining the whole queue before looking loses it). One of those half-rebuilt states is 'HALT and IPL, with no source bit driven yet': a fresh IPL edge from a button nobody pressed, reading the DEFAULT mass memory.
  - evidence: Found with two computers, where it was visible because the second IPL went to a DIFFERENT unit than the panel selected -- GPC2 chose MM2, IPL'd from MM2, then IPL'd again from MM1. MODETRACE showed the mechanism exactly: driven=fc0c0000 (mode bits 0-5 plus 12,13) collapses to driven=80000000 (HALT alone) and returns as driven=f0000000 (bits 0-3, mode but NOT source), and it is that middle state that carries the spurious edge. THEN CONFIRMED PRE-EXISTING IN SINGLE-GPC RUNS, which is the part that matters: headless-gpcmem.sh logs from before the fix contain TWO 'MODE: IPL' lines, 72 blocks each; after the fix, ONE. mmu1 blocksRead falls from 503 to 431, a difference of exactly 72 -- one bootstrap -- and the scripted keystroke lands at simt=133.704 instead of 135.520, the 1.8 s the spurious IPL cost. It was invisible for so long because with one mass memory selected both IPLs read MM1 and the only trace was the extra blocks. FIX: staleness is measured in an ATTENTIVE clock that advances by real time while the machine is polling and by at most 50 ms across a gap when it was not (DISCRETES_ATTEND_MAX_STEP). Same principle the pacer uses for a debugger stall -- time spent not running is not time the world may hold against you.
  - procs `BCE18,BCE19` &middot; env `DISCRETES_STALE_SEC,YAGPC_MODETRACE` &middot; files `src/discretes.c` &middot; symptom `double IPL,second IPL,IPL source reverts to MM1,split bootstrap read,72 extra blocks`
- **#95** *(emulator)* Every computer's INTERCOMPUTER BUS was on the same UDP port -- the first machine's. bcenet_transport_open_bus and the transmit path asked bcenet_bus_port for the port using the TRANSPORT's gpcId instead of the owning machine's, and the transport became one-per-process when the Vehicle took the bus sockets. Bus 24 is the only bus whose port depends on which GPC owns it, and it is exactly the bus a redundant set talks on.
  - evidence: MEASURED, before and after, by opening bus 24 for GPCs 1, 2 and 3 on one transport created as the Vehicle creates it (stamped with GPC1) and listing the bound sockets. BEFORE (tree at 4706c9f6d): '3 :6924' -- all three on one port. AFTER: '1 :6924' and '2 :6925'. The caller had passed the owning gpcId all along (bcenet_framer.c:100 passes f->gpcId); both sites ignored it. The slot now remembers the port it was opened on, because the transmit thread walks slots and cannot otherwise say which computer owns one. Single-computer runs are unchanged by construction: with one machine the transport's id IS the machine's. NOT yet observed in a live run -- bus 24 only opens when the flight software drives the ICC, which needs the redundant set we cannot yet form.
  - procs `BCE24` &middot; env `--bce-network` &middot; files `src/bcenet_transport.c` &middot; symptom `intercomputer bus,ICC,shared port,two GPCs one bus`
- **#97** *(emulator)* The bus-socket slot array was ONE SLOT SHORT, so GPC 5's intercomputer bus wrote off the end of it. BCENET_SLOTS was MAX_BUS_ID+6 where the mapping needs MAX_BUS_ID+1+6: buses 0..24 plus one slot per GPC 0..5. bcenet_slot(24,5) returns 30, and the array had 30 entries.
  - evidence: Found because five computers produced FOUR sockets. Opening bus 24 for GPCs 1-5 on one transport and listing bound ports gave ':6924 :6925 :6926 :6928' -- 6927, GPC5's, missing -- and after sizing the array correctly gives all five. find_bus checked the BUS against BCENET_MAX_BUS_ID but never the computed SLOT, and it is the slot that overran; it now checks the slot. NOT a multi-GPC-only defect: --gpc-id 5 --bce-network reached the same out-of-bounds write with a single computer, since the slot depends on the GPC id and not on how many are running.
  - procs `BCE24` &middot; env `--bce-network,--gpc-id` &middot; files `src/bcenet_transport.c` &middot; symptom `out of bounds,slot,GPC5,missing socket,intercomputer bus`
- **#98** *(emulator)* A display unit can now be ATTACHED TO A COMPUTER, because two GPCs cannot drive the same one: a display-keyboard bus has a single commander and a unit hangs off one bus. --deu-bus takes <gpc>:<bus>; the built-in DK1 unit belongs to the computer that created it, which is the first one named; a computer with no unit attached finds nothing on the display buses, which is the truth about a GPC with no MEDS on it.
  - evidence: Before: two computers both driving DK1 abandoned 477 transfers and no keystroke reached the flight software. After, with --gpcs 1,2 --deu-model --deu-bus 2:7 on headless-gpcmem.sh's own timings: ZERO clashes on any bus, and GPC1's keystroke lands at poll=247 simt=133.704 s wall=120.0 s -- the single-GPC figure exactly. ONE TRAP WORTH THE NOTE: the owner must be stamped unconditionally when the unit is created, NOT gated on nMachines>1, because nMachines is still 1 at that moment even in a five-computer run -- the other machines have not reached batchrunner_init yet. Gated, the unit was owned by nobody and every computer drove it: 184 clashes on bus 6 in a run that was supposed to have none.
  - procs `DK1,DK2` &middot; env `--deu-bus,YAGPC_DEUKEYS` &middot; files `src/run.c,src/run.h,src/vehicle.h,src/opts.c` &middot; symptom `abandoned transfers,no keystroke delivered,DK bus assignment,shared display unit`
- **#106** *(flight-sw)* THE COMMON SET NOW FORMS. FCMASYNC detects its neighbour, exits its search loop on an observed SIP, and FCMSMASK writes a NON-EMPTY mask -- TCVTCSSM is 0x888 on GPC1 and 0x111 on GPC2, each the other's rotated position. This entry previously recorded the opposite, that FCMSMASK was called once per machine with an empty set and ARCGPC's FORM RS was never reached; that was a true observation of a BROKEN state, and the break was the stale-level defect in #110, not anything in the flight software.
  - evidence: Run cs3, watching the fullword at 0x000168 across a 900 s two-computer run with the level fix in place: 'gpc=1 addr=00169 val=00000888' and 'gpc=2 addr=00169 val=00000111', each alongside the earlier zero. 0x888 is bits 20, 24 and 28 -- the N+1 group, which from GPC1's seat is GPC2 -- and 0x111 is bits 23, 27 and 31, the N+4 group, which from GPC2's seat is GPC1. So each computer's common set names the other. The detection itself is visible in the instruction trace (run fa6): FCMASYNC's eligibility test 'NR 2,0' at 0x18085 yields R2=00000100, bit 23, GPC1 at N+4, and the loop exits there instead of running to its 170 ms timeout. Before the fix that test only ever produced zero. THE GOAL IS STILL NOT REACHED: GPC2 does not transition, mmu2 ending at 59 commands / 432 blocks against GPC1's 71 / 749, so what remains is downstream of common-set formation rather than in it.
  - addrs `196ba-196ba,00168-00169,02406-02407` &middot; csects `FCMSMASK,ARCGPC,DM6OPS,DM5NEW` &middot; instrs `SVC 38` &middot; tape `v44` &middot; symptom `no redundant set,TCVTCSSM zero,FORM RS never runs,OPS 2 loads but no set` &middot; run `cs1,cs2`
- **#110** *(emulator)* THE INTER-GPC LINES ARE LEVELS AND WE TREATED THEM AS EVENTS, so a neighbour's steady bits went stale and its code was read WRONG. A computer holds STBY and RUN set for as long as it runs and toggles only SYNC; routing the lines on CHANGE alone refreshed the toggling bit and let the two steady ones age out of the neighbour's driven mask after DISCRETES_STALE_SEC. They then fell back to the neighbour's locally derived value, which is zero, so a null code of 111 was read by the flight software as 001.
  - evidence: Caught by tracing FCMASYNC itself (0x18052-0x18100) on the SECOND computer. Its DIA read -- PC 2,6 with the command word 08180000, which is READ DISCRETE INPUT A -- returned R2=27000001: bit 31 set, bits 23 and 27 CLEAR, i.e. GPC1 reported as code 001 when GPC1 only ever emits 111 and 110. AFTER re-asserting each computer's whole rotated code to its neighbours on the discrete-poll schedule, the same read returns R2=27000111, bits 23, 27 and 31 all present -- the correct null. AND THE TRAFFIC CHANGED: a code beyond null and SSIP appears for the first time in this work, 8 instances of 010 IPR, so the computers are now reacting to each other rather than talking past each other. WHY THE EARLIER MEASUREMENT MISSED IT: the driven mask was sampled in the sync trace, which only prints ON A CHANGE -- exactly the instant all three bits had just been refreshed -- so it read fc0c0111 and looked correct. A level signal has to be checked between its transitions, not at them. TWO TRACE DEFECTS FIXED ALONG THE WAY, both of which had hidden this: YAGPC_RANGETRACE's line budget is a file static, so the first computer to reach the range spent all of it and the second machine's pass was never seen (YAGPC_RANGETRACE_GPC now restricts it), and its afterSec gate is in each machine's OWN simulated time, so a window chosen from wall-clock timings silently excluded the later-starting computer. addrs 18052-18100 | csects FCMASYNC,FCMCOLAG | env YAGPC_RANGETRACE,YAGPC_RANGETRACE_GPC,DISCRETES_STALE_SEC | files src/vehicle.c,src/vehicle.h,src/run.c | symptom neighbour code read wrong,001 instead of 111,stale level,common set | run fa3,fa4  FOLLOW-UP MEASUREMENT, run fa5: tracing ONLY the DIA instruction at 0x180e7 on GPC2, with a budget big enough not to truncate, gives 2300 samples inside FCMCOLAG -- 2293 reading 27000111 (null) and SEVEN reading 27000110, the SSIP code. So the second computer now OBSERVES its neighbour in SIP, which the stale-level defect had made impossible. Seven in 2300 is 0.3%, exactly the duty cycle of a 330 us pulse every 160 ms, so the sampling is working as designed rather than by luck. A caution for anyone reading a range trace here: the earlier attempt at this count gave 115 samples and was MEANINGLESS -- the 3000-line budget had been exhausted, and 115 was simply how many FCMCOLAG iterations fit in it, not how many ran.
  - addrs `18052-18100` &middot; csects `FCMASYNC,FCMCOLAG` &middot; env `YAGPC_RANGETRACE,YAGPC_RANGETRACE_GPC,DISCRETES_STALE_SEC` &middot; files `src/vehicle.c,src/vehicle.h,src/run.c` &middot; symptom `neighbour code read wrong,001 instead of 111,stale level,common set` &middot; run `fa3,fa4`
- **#118** *(emulator)* THE ROTATED CODE IS NOW APPLIED INDIVISIBLY, set and clear in one locked step. It is a real hazard -- half a three-bit code is a different code -- but it is NOT what fails the sync: with the fix the admit-to-vote-out window is unchanged at 4.33 ms and GPC2 still does not transition.
  - evidence: vehicle_route_out and vehicle_refresh_lines each applied the set mask and the clear mask as two separately-locked calls, so a neighbour reading discrete input A between them saw a torn code. discretes_apply_external_pair now does both under one lock. Run at1, identical to cs4: CZ2B_CS still goes 0x0008 at t=435.2097 s and back to zero at t=435.2140 s, mmu1 71/749 and mmu2 59/432 exactly as before. A CORRECTION TO WHY THIS WAS TRIED, and it is the point worth keeping: I read the failing FCMISYNC passes as each computer waiting on TWO of its neighbour's three bits -- GPC1 with R4=0x088 against a mask of 0x888, GPC2 with R4=0x011 against 0x111 -- and took that for a torn code. It is not. 0x088 is TCVTSVCI, the SVC code-inverse SENDING PATTERN from MLIB80/TFCVT.asm, a CONSTANT of the sync protocol and not a mask at all. Reading a known constant as evidence is the same class of error as reading a truncated trace count as a rate (#110) or an entry count as a barrier count (#117), and it is the third instance in this work. CHECK A SUSPICIOUS REGISTER VALUE AGAINST TFCVT'S CONSTANT TABLE BEFORE BUILDING ON IT.
  - csects `FCMISYNC` &middot; files `src/discretes.c,src/discretes.h,src/vehicle.c` &middot; symptom `torn code,partial sync code,atomic apply,fail to sync` &middot; run `at1`
- **#121** *(emulator)* A COMPUTER DENIED ITS NEIGHBOUR'S DISPLAY FELL THROUGH TO THE ROUTER'S FALLBACK. The ownership check for a --deu-bus unit used a BREAK statement, which left the bus UNMATCHED, and an unmatched bus falls through to `fallback` -- the servicer installed before the router, which is the BUILT-IN display under --deu-model and the NETWORK FRAMER under --bce-network. CORRECTED 2026-09-13: the first statement of this entry claimed the fallback "answers on ANY bus number". Under --deu-model it does NOT: deumodel.c:637 tests `input->busID != d->busID` and returns a no-peripheral answer, bit for bit what bus_no_peripheral returns. So in the dk2/dk3 runs, which were --deu-model, the flight software saw the SAME answer before and after the fix, and the 561,010 figure is ATTEMPTED transactions counted by vehicle_bus_enter in the fallback path before the model declined -- not transactions the model served. The fix is still right (it stops taking bus 7's lock and polluting the census, and it is load-bearing under --bce-network, where the framer really would have put a denied computer's traffic on the wire), but it did not change what the software was told.
  - evidence: Bus census of a two-computer run with --deu-bus 2:7 (run dk2): 'bus 7 transactions: GPC1=561010' with GPC2 absent, and the FCMISYNC ratio unchanged from the run without the unit at all -- 34,432 against 487, digit for digit. After returning a no-peripheral answer instead of breaking (run dk3), bus 7 vanishes from the census entirely: GPC1 is correctly denied AND GPC2 still never asks. SO THE DISPLAY DOES NOT FIX THE SET, AND THE RATIO IS NOT CAUSED BY ITS ABSENCE -- 47,778 against 487 afterwards. CORRECTED 2026-09-13: that dk2-to-dk3 movement was attributed here to "the software denied DK2 pushes its traffic elsewhere". It cannot be. The built-in DEU declined bus 7 in BOTH runs, so the software was denied DK2 either way and the only difference the fix made was the census count and one mutex; the 34k-to-48k movement is therefore run-to-run variation or lock overhead, NOT a response to the denial, and nothing should be built on it. GPC2 never commands the unit assigned to it because the NBAT assignment takes effect only at a transition GPC2 cannot reach, which is the circularity of #114 and is unchanged. WHAT THE CENSUS SHOWS INSTEAD: the inflation is dominated by buses with no model at all -- 14 and 15 at 3.5 million each (FC5 and FC6), 20 at 5.1 million, 21 at 3.0 million, 24 at 2.5 million. Buses 1, 2, 8, 10 and 11 have traffic for the same reason: they are ATTEMPTS counted at the router with nothing answering, which is #49/#115's unserved-bus inflation, not a display model replying where it should not. MEDS2.py cannot behave the way the retracted sentence described in any case -- it binds one UDP socket per bus (MEDS2.py:360, one port per bus in busConfig) and an IDP subscribes only to the buses in its own `busses` list, so a DK2 IDP never sees a DK1 datagram at all.
  - procs `DK1,DK2` &middot; env `--deu-bus,YAGPC_BUSCENSUS` &middot; files `src/run.c,src/deumodel.c` &middot; symptom `deu-bus assignment ignored,fallback serves any bus,70 to 1,census counts attempts not replies` &middot; run `dk2,dk3`
- **#122** *(emulator)* THE INTERCOMPUTER BUS WAS NEVER WIRED TO GPC 1, so the two computers had NO DATA PATH BETWEEN THEM AT ALL. batchrunner_init increments veh->nMachines at its top and then, 260 lines later, decides whether to build the ICC with `veh->icc == NULL && veh->nMachines > 1`. While the FIRST machine is being built that counter reads 1, so the test is false for GPC 1: the wire is created by GPC 2 and only GPC 2's router ever holds a pointer to it. GPC 1 does all the intercomputer traffic, so its bus-24 transmissions fell through to the display fallback, which declined every one.
  - evidence: Run ms2, 900 s, --gpcs 1,2, the ICC model's own closing report: icc: {"xmitCmds":0,"xmitWords":0,"recvPolls":0,"recvCalls":0,"recvWords":0,"pending":0,"dropped":0}. Zero on every counter, including recvCalls, which iccmodel.c carries under the comment "does anybody even ask?". THIS IS WHERE #115's HEADLINE NUMBER CAME FROM: the 2,036,905 bus-24 transactions it recorded for GPC1 are router-level ATTEMPTS counted by vehicle_bus_enter in the fallback path, into a wire that was not connected -- not intercomputer traffic, and not evidence of dispatch inflation on that bus. Same shape as the error corrected in #121: the census measures the ROUTER, and only the model knows whether anything was served. THE TRAP IS OLDER THAN THIS DEFECT. The identical initialisation-order mistake had already been found and fixed IN THE SAME FUNCTION for the display unit -- "nMachines is still 1 here even in a five-computer run, because the others have not reached batchrunner_init yet... Gating on it left the unit owned by nobody and every computer drove it" -- and that fix was local, so the trap stayed open for the next decision made during the first machine's construction. FIX: main() now calls vehicle_expect_machines(&vehicle, nGpc) before any BatchRunner is built, Vehicle carries nExpected, and vehicle_multi() prefers it, so it is correct DURING construction as well as after; vehicle_bus_enter/leave, the barrier and vehicle_add_machine were moved onto vehicle_multi() for the same reason. The ICC build site now asks vehicle_multi(veh). WHAT THIS DOES NOT YET SHOW: whether connecting the wire makes the common set hold. That is being measured.
  - procs `BCE24` &middot; env `YAGPC_ICCTRACE,YAGPC_BUSCENSUS,--gpcs` &middot; files `src/run.c,src/main.c,src/vehicle.c,src/vehicle.h,src/iccmodel.c` &middot; symptom `no intercomputer data path,icc model untouched,fail to sync,set voted apart,initialisation order,nMachines` &middot; run `ms2`
- **#123** *(emulator)* ANSWERED AND FIXED: THE INTERCOMPUTER BUSES ARE 1-5, ONE PER COMPUTER, AND yaGPC2 HAD THE ICC ON BUS 24. Bus 24 is the IP bus and carries something else entirely. Settled not by the bus table's names but by the flight software's own command words, which match SIPICCNT to the word and show each computer commanding ITS OWN bus. The ICC model now serves buses 1-5 with queues per (bus, receiver), because a computer COMMANDS its own bus and LISTENS on the other four -- so several independent conversations exist at once and a single shared wire ran them into each other.
  - evidence: Run fx-cmd, 400 s, --gpcs 1,2, YAGPC_CMDTRACE decoding every command word on buses 1-5 and 24 (iua = bits 5-9, func = 13-22, count = 23-31 plus one, per BCE Principles of Operation IBM-6246556A part 3). The whole of it: "CMD gpc=1 bus=1 iua=5 func=000 words=124" x2; "CMD gpc=2 bus=2 iua=5 func=000 words=124" x2; and on bus 24, "gpc=1 iua=13 func=120/122/124/126 words=32" x7134 each plus "gpc=1 iua=15 func=221 words=481" x6147 and func=226 x987. 124 IS SIPICCNT, the SSIP ICC word count declared in FIOICCPG.asm, exactly; GPC1 commands bus 1 and GPC2 commands bus 2, which is the bus table's IC1 and IC2 and each computer's own; and bus 24 carries 32-word and 481-word reads from two other interface units and nothing of ICC shape at all. The bus census agrees: bus 1 GPC1=12396, bus 2 GPC1=8, with nothing modelling either, so every one of those was declined by the fallback. WHY THE LISTENER NEVER RAN, which was #122's leftover: GPC2's listener would read BUS 1, and bus 1 had no model, so #RDLI got "nothing available" forever while the model on bus 24 filled with traffic nobody had asked for -- 909,696 words transmitted, 0 read, 3.7 million dropped. FIX: YAGPC_ICC_BUS 24 becomes YAGPC_ICC_BUS_FIRST 1 / _LAST 5 / YAGPC_ICC_IS_BUS(); IccModel's queues become q[bus][receiver]; icc_broadcast takes the bus; the report breaks down per bus, because "the ICC works" is five questions and a bus with transmits and no reads names which computer nobody is listening to. Bus 24 now has no model, which is the truth about a bus whose device we do not simulate. METHOD NOTE: the naming alone was NOT sufficient evidence and was not used as such -- PASS puts 812,773 transactions on bus 24 against 12,396 on bus 1, so the busier bus was the wrong one, and only the command words separated them.
  - csects `FIOCBLKS,FIOICCPG` &middot; procs `BCE1,BCE2,BCE3,BCE4,BCE5,BCE24` &middot; env `YAGPC_CMDTRACE,YAGPC_BUSCENSUS,YAGPC_ICCTRACE` &middot; files `src/iccmodel.c,src/iccmodel.h,src/run.c,src/vehicle.h` &middot; symptom `intercomputer bus numbering,IC vs IP,bus table conflation,shared port` &middot; run `fx-icc,fx-cmd`
- **#127** *(emulator)* A HELD MACHINE NOW SPINS BRIEFLY AND IS THEN WOKEN BY ITS PARTNER'S PROGRESS, INSTEAD OF SLEEPING ON A TIMER, AND THAT MAKES A TIGHT BARRIER CHEAP. Per hold cost fell from ~115 us to 6-7 us, 99.5% of holds end while spinning, and the pair covers 2.3 times as much joint emulated time in the same wall time. It does not by itself stop the pair being voted apart; the remaining votes follow ICC checksum failures (#128).
  - evidence: Three 450 s two-computer runs at YAGPC_BARRIER_US=25 on the new barrier (wk-b25-f1-3, -f1-5, -f2-4) against four 450 s runs on the old one (tr-b25-*, which carried a light range trace -- some of the gap is that). OLD: 3.08-3.13M holds, 359-361 s held, 115-117 us each, joint emulated time 96-99 s, first vote 7.7-22.7 s in. NEW: 22.9-24.6M holds, 152-160 s held, 6-7 us each, 99.5-99.6% released while spinning, 169-178k sleeps, 0 abandoned, joint emulated time 222-224 s, first vote 2.6, 20.2 and 37.8 s in. Joint time is to the last sync-code change, the same measure for both. HOW: vehicle_barrier_wait re-checks for YAGPC_BARRIER_SPIN_US (default 200) of wall time, then sleeps on barCond with a 2 ms safety timeout; the slowest machine, which publishes its time every instruction, broadcasts when that time reaches barWakeAtUs, and a machine leaving the barrier broadcasts too. The publish-before-check and register-before-recheck ordering means a waker and a sleeper cannot miss each other. REGRESSION GATE (single GPC, wk-regress): every event counter exact -- mmu1 commands 56, blocksRead 431, wordsOut 220731, wordsTaken 220011, wordsLost 720, position 3/3/0; deu formatFills 8, resets 1, modeStatus 6, ipled true, error counters 0 -- and periodic counters in today's range (deu commands 1846, mtu 7890). A single-computer run returns before any of the new code, as vehicle_multi() is false.
  - env `YAGPC_BARRIER_US,YAGPC_BARRIER_SPIN_US` &middot; files `src/vehicle.c,src/vehicle.h` &middot; symptom `barrier cost,hold latency,wake on progress,condition variable,emulated time per wall time` &middot; run `wk-b25-f1-3,wk-b25-f1-5,wk-b25-f2-4,tr-b25-f1-0,wk-regress`
- **#128** *(emulator)* THE FAILURES LEFT AT A TIGHT BARRIER ARE ICC CHECKSUM ERRORS: an intercomputer transfer intermittently arrives with contents that do not match its checksum, the flight software retries it once, and when the retry fails too it reports an input problem on that I/O while the partner reports success. The two computers then disagree at the I/O-complete barrier and vote each other out. Since the ICC wire is entirely this emulator's model, the corruption is presumed to be ours until shown otherwise.
  - evidence: Runs tr-b25-* (range trace of FIOCMPLT's call sites into FCMISYNC, named with the FIOIAT table from DASS_G2 -- I/O numbers 1-15 at 6764 6772 904C 905A 9068 9076 9084 9092 A0D6 A0E4 A0F2 A100 A10E 6780 678E) and ie-b25-* (YAGPC_WATCHHW=908b on IOQE7's TIOQFLG1, plus a trace of FIOCMPLT's code-selection branch at 0x1b4d9-0x1b4df). WHICH I/O: in all four tr runs the completions flagged IPR at the vote are IOQE7 @9084, device 3, the ICC request with TIOQSYNT set -- 3-12 of each computer's 53-159 ICC completions carry IPR, and at the vote one computer's last ICC completions are IPR while its partner's are IOC. WHICH ERROR: of the IPR-flagged ICC completions in ie-b25-f1-0, 5 (GPC1) and 8 (GPC2) came through the checksum mask FIOTMASK and 1 each through the error/pseudo-timeout flag; ie-b25-f1-2 and ie-b25-f2-1 the same shape. The checksum-error bit TIOQCSER (X'2000') is set at FIOCMPLT+00A8, SB 7(R1),X'2000', reached when FIOTMASK AND NOT FIOBMASK is non-zero, followed by LH R3,TCVTIPRI+1. THE RETRY: FIOCMPLT+03B8..+03C6 tests TIOQICCR, and if TIOQIRTY is not yet set sets it, clears X'2098' (CSER, TRER, ERPR, PSTO) and restarts the bus programs with PC R3,R7 on the monitored-bus mask -- 13 retries on EACH computer in ie-b25-f1-0, symmetric, so both see bad checksums at the same rate. A separate curiosity, not the pair-breaker: on the computer that runs the vehicle's buses, every completion of IOQE4 (device 24) is IPR, 1,267 of 1,267, but those are redundant-set I/Os and cost nothing at the barrier. SUSPECT, NOT YET MEASURED: iccmodel queues the commander's COMMAND word to every listener as if it were data -- the receive path carries no sync type -- where a real receiver distinguishes command sync from data sync.  FOUND AND FIXED 2026-09-13. CAUSE: iccmodel queued the commander's COMMAND word to every listener like a data word. Tagging every word with its transfer number and position (YAGPC_ICCSEQ, runs sq-b25-*) showed listeners took the command word as data on 15 of 16 transfers in one run and 256 of 260 in another, never skipped a position and never left a transfer early. A listener reads SIPICCNT=124 words, the commander puts 125 on the wire, so each transfer left one data word behind for the next -- 1,860 words read for 15 transfers, 124 each -- and the copy of the partner's ICC buffer slid a word further out of register every cycle. WHY ONLY SOME FAIL: FIOCKSM (FIOCMPLT.asm) checksums a partner's buffer only when TICCSIPO AND 3 equals 1, 'no cycle overrun'; on shifted data that first word is arbitrary, so the check usually skips and occasionally runs over garbage -- FIOICCCK sums TICCVBLG halfwords of TICCBUFF, the variable length itself being garbage -- and miscompares against TICCCKSM. HARDWARE AUTHORITY: BCE Principles of Operation (IBM-6246556A part 3) section 3.4.4 -- the MIA supplies each word's sync type, and a BCE in command mode 'will check to see if the first input had command sync, and if so, it will ignore the input, reset the timer to MTO, and await the first input. This procedure allows a BCE that has just transmitted a command to skip over the copy of that command'. A command word is never stored as data. FIX: the command word is no longer delivered to listeners. VERIFIED (runs fx2-*): at YAGPC_BARRIER_US=25 all four 450 s runs had NO fail-vote over 211-222 s of joint emulated time, 1,321-1,386 ICC transfers each way, symmetric, 0 word-order anomalies. At the default 200 us all four still voted apart, first votes at 2.4, 6.7, 20.3 and 78.1 s -- the barrier-freeze handshake failure of #126 -- so BOTH this fix and a tight barrier are needed for the common set to hold.
  - addrs `1b4d9-1b4df,1b528-1b52b,1b838-1b846,09084-0908c` &middot; csects `FIOCMPLT,FIOICCPG,FCMISYNC,FIOCBLKS` &middot; env `YAGPC_WATCHHW,YAGPC_RANGETRACE,YAGPC_BARRIER_US` &middot; files `src/iccmodel.c,src/iop.c` &middot; symptom `ICC checksum,IPR,input problem report,retry,IOQE7,disagreement at I/O complete,command word as data` &middot; run `tr-b25-f1-0,tr-b25-f1-2,tr-b25-f2-1,tr-b25-f2-3,ie-b25-f1-0,ie-b25-f1-2,ie-b25-f2-1`
- **#131** *(emulator)* EVERY ICC TRANSFER AFTER THE FIRST WAS READ ONE SSIP CYCLE LATE, IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, BECAUSE THE ICC MODEL QUEUED WORDS SENT WHILE NOBODY WAS LISTENING. At common-set formation the commander sends two transfers 1.9 ms apart; the listener has finished the first and is not listening for the second; the model kept the second, the next cycle's listen took it, and from then on every listen took the previous cycle's transfer. A real bus holds nothing. Consequence: each computer processes its partner's ICC buffer a cycle late, which is why GPC2 acts on the OPS transition's type-2 message only after GPC1 has formed the redundant set and voted (#129).
  - evidence: Runs lag-b25-0..2, 420 s, two computers, YAGPC_ICCLAG timing each transfer on the shared clock from the sender's last word to each reader's last word. All three identical: bus 1 read by GPC2, 954 transfers, 953 in the 150-170 ms bin (max 159,885 us) and 1 under 10 ms; bus 2 read by GPC1 the same. Per transfer: transfer 1 sent 266,168,656 us, read at 266,170,306 (lag 1,650 us); transfer 2 sent 266,170,570 -- 1.9 ms after transfer 1 -- and read at 266,328,128 (lag 157,559 us); transfers 3-30 lag 159.7-159.9 ms, each read ~200 us before the next transfer is sent. The 'older words still queued at a new command' counter read 0, and queued_after was 0 on every read: METHOD NOTE, that counter looks at the wrong moment, because each listen drains the backlog just before the next command arrives, so a permanent one- transfer lag never shows as a queue at command time -- only the per- transfer lag reveals it. FIX (runs ex-*): an ICC word not taken within YAGPC_ICC_EXPIRE_US (default 10,000) of being sent, on the shared clock, expires before a receive can see it; with no barrier there is no shared clock and nothing expires.  RESOLUTION: the lag was the visible end of #133 -- the listener timed out on its first arm, the retry's words were kept by the model's queue, and every later listen took the previous transfer. With Listen Mode implemented the lag is under 1 ms on every transfer. The expiry rule (YAGPC_ICC_EXPIRE_US, default 10 ms) is kept: a bus holds nothing, and without it a Listen-Mode receiver could wake on a command left over from an earlier cycle.
  - csects `AIESIP,FIOICCPG` &middot; env `YAGPC_ICCLAG,YAGPC_ICC_EXPIRE_US,YAGPC_BARRIER_US` &middot; files `src/iccmodel.c,src/run.c` &middot; symptom `ICC one cycle late,stale partner buffer,queued words on a bus,OPS transition,TSIP message late` &middot; run `lag-b25-0,lag-b25-1,lag-b25-2`
- **#132** *(emulator)* THE ICC LISTENER IS ARMED WITH A 33 US MESSAGE TIMEOUT AND TIMES OUT BEFORE THE COMMANDER'S FIRST WORD, EVERY CYCLE. FCMINIOP loads the ICC listener BCE an MTO of 2 ticks; the listener's #RDLI of 124 words (pc 199DA) arms, gets nothing within 33 us, and error-terminates; the IOP marks the ICC request TRER, FCOS retries it, the listener re-arms 1.8 ms later and reads the words then waiting, and the commander's retransmission expires unread. With the model's old stale queue this was invisible except at formation -- the first arm always found last cycle's words -- and that single formation timeout is what started the permanent one-cycle lag (#131). A real listener cannot meet a 33 us first-word deadline while the commander delays 198 us (#DLYI 12) either, so the hardware must synchronise them by another route; Listen Mode (#WIX waiting for a listen command) is the prime suspect, and the command-word fix (#128) may have removed that very word.
  - evidence: Runs to-b25-0/1 (expiry build, YAGPC_TIMEOUT_TRACE with the new YAGPC_TIMEOUT_TRACE_PE=1,2 filter). Both runs identical: 'BCE1/BCE2 count=124 mto=2 timeout=0.03 ms pc=199da' x4 each; 'BCE1/BCE2 RECV TIMEOUT left=124 gotAny=0 waited=0.03 ms mto=0.03 ms' x2 each; e.g. GPC2 BCE2 armed at 265,208,453.5 us, timed out at 265,208,486.5 (33 us), re- armed at 265,210,367.5 (1.9 ms later) and read. The receive floor is 0 because run.c sets it to 0 with --deu-model ('honour the bus program's own message timeout exactly'). Preceding runs er-b25-0/1: the IOP writes PREI+TRER into IOQE7's TIOQFLG1 every cycle (76 times in 38 cycles), FIOCMPLT+03C1 sets IRTY 38 times on each computer, and the completion path is 'IPR via error/pseudo-timeout' 38 times against 'via checksum mask' once -- so the per-cycle error is the timeout, not a checksum. #DLYI verified correct: MTO_TICK_US = 16.5 matches the BCE Principles of Operation, 'a resolution of 16.5 microseconds per count'. The expiry change (uncommitted) is therefore not a fix on its own: it removes the lag and exposes the timeout, and the pair is voted apart within ~8 cycles.  THE MTO OF 2 IS AS-BUILT, NOT AN EMULATOR LOAD ERROR: FCMINIOP.asm:156-162 loads 'L R4,FCMTMB1 TIME BUSES 1-5 33 MICS' into buses 1-5 through PC commands starting at FCMTBCD X'A2158000' 'COMMAND WORD TO WRITE BANK B BUS 1', stepping 16 per bus, and FCMTMB1 is 'DC F''2'' 33 MICS-2 SPINS'; the same value goes to buses 10-11, 14-17 and 20-23, with 303 (5 ms) for 6-9, 37 (610.5 us) for 12-13, 118784 (1.96 s) for 18-19 and 3 (49.5 us) for 24. The emulator's #LTO/#LTOI were checked and read full 18-bit counts. So a real ICC listener is also armed with a 33 us first-word timeout while its commander delays 198 us before sending, which can only work if the listener arms later than the commander by about that delay, or the first-input timer does not run while the bus is silent -- the next thing to establish.
  - addrs `199da-199da` &middot; csects `FIOICCPG,FIOCMPLT,FCMINIOP` &middot; procs `BCE1,BCE2` &middot; env `YAGPC_TIMEOUT_TRACE,YAGPC_TIMEOUT_TRACE_PE,YAGPC_ICC_EXPIRE_US,YAGPC_WATCHHW` &middot; files `src/iop.c,src/iccmodel.c,src/iop_bce_instr.c` &middot; symptom `ICC listener timeout,MTO 2 ticks,first input timeout,TRER every cycle,retry,listen mode,WIX` &middot; run `to-b25-0,to-b25-1,er-b25-0,er-b25-1`
- **#133** *(emulator)* yaGPC2's RECEIVE INSTRUCTIONS DO NOT IMPLEMENT LISTEN MODE, AND THAT IS WHY THE ICC LISTENER TIMES OUT. Per the BCE Principles of Operation, a BCE whose MIA transmitter is disabled is in Listen Mode, and its #RDS/#RDLI/#RDL/#MIN receive 'waits (indefinitely) for a bus word having command sync ... and an IUA that matches the BCE's IUAR', starting its MTO timer only when that command arrives. The emulator starts the MTO timer immediately, which is Command Mode behaviour, so the ICC listener -- MTO 2, 33 us, as-built -- times out ~200 us before its commander's first word. This also CORRECTS #128: dropping the command word from listeners removed the very word a Listen-Mode receiver needs as its trigger. The right model delivers the command word MARKED as command sync, and the receiver handles it according to its mode.
  - evidence: BCE Principles of Operation (IBM-6246556A part 3), section 4.1 Listen Mode Configuration, verbatim: 'Execution of #RDS, #RDLI, #RDL, and the receive part of #MIN also differ between Command and Listen Mode, primarily in accepting the first data input. In Command Mode, when these instructions enter the receive algorithm, they immediately start waiting for the first data word, and timing out its arrival by decrementing a timer that had been set to the value in the MTO Register. If the first input the BCE finds in the MIA Buffer is a command, the BCE will discard the command, reset the timer, and start looking for data again. This allows the BCE to ignore the command that was sent to the subsystem but was also echoed back by the MIA to the BCE's MIA Buffer. A BCE in Listen Mode goes through a different sequence. Rather than immediately waiting for data, the BCE waits (indefinitely) for a bus word having command sync, good parity, etc., and an IUA that matches the BCE's IUAR. This command should be the command being sent by the other BCE/IOP in Command Mode to the subsystem, telling it to return data. The BCE uses the arrival of this command as a signal to set its timers and start watching for arrival of the first input of data. This allows the Listening BCE to accurately time out the first data word, and detect if that word is excessively delayed in its arrival. After arrival of the first word all BCEs use the same reception algorithm.' Section 2.2.3.1: 'In the Listen Mode the BCE's MIA receiver is enabled, but the BCE's MIA transmitter is disabled. In Command mode both transmitter and receiver are enabled.' Section 3.4.4: a Listen-Mode BCE treats any other first- input discrepancy as an error; a Command-Mode BCE skips one command-sync echo. The flight image fits this exactly: FIOSICLS at 0x199DA is '#LBR@ FIOBRE; #RDLI 123', FIOSICCM at 0x199CE is '#LBR@; #DLYI 0; #DLYI 12; #MOUT 0,123 with command word 0028 007B', and FCMINIOP.asm:199-211 sets the IUAR of the ICC BCEs to 5 ('SET ICC IUA') -- the IUA of command 28007B. Measured in the emulator (runs to-b25-0/1): listener armed at +161,669.662 ms, timed out at +161,669.695 ms (33 us), the commander's words sent at +161,669.872 ms, 177 us after the listener gave up; re-  FIXED AND VERIFIED 2026-09-13. Implementation: received words carry command sync in their top bit (src/busword.h), set by the ICC model on command words, which it delivers again; the MIA strips the bit into MIA.lastCmdSync; iop_bce_receive arms a Listen-Mode wait when the BCE's transmitter is disabled and the bus's model marks sync (IOP.busMarksSync, bits 1-5 when the ICC model is installed), skips words until a command-sync word whose IUA matches the IUARs arrives, and only then starts the MTO; Command Mode skips one command-sync echo; any other command-sync word error-terminates. Buses whose models do not mark sync keep the old behaviour. RESULTS, runs lm-lag-0/1 (420 s): every ICC listener arm is listen=1 (853 and 606 per BCE); timeouts 0 and 1; ICC lag under 1 ms on 853/853 and 1,211/1,212 transfers (min -19 us, max 34 us, where it was ~159.8 ms); one transfer per cycle, no retries; NO fail-votes in either run. Runs lm-ops-2/3/4 (720 s, full OPS 201 sequence): type-2 message read in the same cycle it was sent (615.2883 sent, 615.2883 read); SVC syncs (100) 136/126, 136/127, 133/122 and timer syncs (101) 10/10 on both computers -- the first redundant-set syncs ever seen; the pair is still voted apart and cleared later in all three. Regression gate (lm-regress, single GPC): every event counter exact, periodic deu 1848/375/569/367/575 and mtu 7897.
  - addrs `199ce-199ed` &middot; csects `FIOICCPG,FCMINIOP,FIOADCCL` &middot; procs `BCE1,BCE2` &middot; files `src/iop.c,src/iop_bce_instr.c,src/iccmodel.c` &middot; symptom `listen mode,command mode,first input timeout,MTO,IUAR,command sync,ICC listener,correction to #128` &middot; run `to-b25-0,to-b25-1`
- **#138** *(emulator)* A LISTENING BCE THAT HAS NEVER COMMANDED ITS BUS WAITS FOREVER, BECAUSE OUR #CMDI AND #CMD LOAD THE IUAR ONLY WHEN THE TRANSMITTER IS ENABLED. FIOPRMPG's timing-unit listener runs '#CMDI FIOFFIUA,FIOMDMRT  SET IUA.' then '#RDLI 6' with its transmitter off; that #CMDI exists only to give the Listen-Mode receive its address. Skipped, the IUAR stays 0, the receive never sees a matching command, the BCE stays busy, and the MSC's single look reports the I/O in error on that computer alone -- which then fails itself out of the set.
  - evidence: Run w7-short-0 (display-bus wire hold on, gpc-labelled RECV ARM with iuar=, REPEAT): the break after the wire hold is GPC2's self- FTS from FIOERRLC+0313 with R3=90c4 at 467.495 s; GPC1 completes 90c4 clean (0880) and GPC2 with the IPR code (0808); the FIOMCKIO mask before both is 00000e00 = buses 20, 21, 22 (the MTU read). At the transaction GPC1 arms BCE20 and BCE21 as commander (xmit=1) and BCE22 as listener (listen=1, iuar=10 -- left from when GPC1 commanded bus 22), and GPC2 arms BCE22 as commander; GPC2's listeners on 20 and 21 armed ONCE, at 466.090 s (BCE20, pc 1cc2e, listen=1, iuar=0) and 466.530 s (BCE21, pc 1ccae, count 7, listen=1, iuar=0), and never again -- they were still waiting a second later. SOURCE: FIOPRMPG.asm LISTENER CODE FOR MTU READ: 'FIOEL51L ... #CMDI FIOFFIUA,FIOMDMRT SET IUA. / #RDLI 6 RECEIVE DATA.' EMULATOR: exec_CMDI and exec_CMD set iopls_IUAR inside 'if (iop_proc_get(&t->regXmitEna, t->curPE))'. BCE POO: '#CMDI and #CMD ... The execution of such instructions also sets the BCE's Interface Unit Address Register (IUAR) to whatever IUA is specified in the command', and for a busy MIA or disabled transmitter 'the command is aborted, but no error condition is set' -- execution, not transmission, loads the register; #MIN is the documented exception ('In Listen mode, the IUAR is unchanged'). ALSO VERIFIED: the display-bus wire hold as the default with several computers (commit 8ba74c464) reproduces the opt-in run exactly (dh-short-0 = wh-dk-0) and passes the gate (dh-regress). FIX UNDER TEST: #CMDI/#CMD load the IUAR unconditionally and transmit only when enabled; runs ci-regress, ci-short-0, ci-std-0. FIXED AND VERIFIED: #CMDI and #CMD now load the IUAR whether or not the transmitter is enabled, transmitting only when it is. Regression gate ci-regress: EVERY EVENT COUNTER EXACT, periodic unchanged. Two-computer run ci-short-0 (OPS at 450 s): the redundant set formed at 465.59 s and ran in lockstep for more than 30 s -- 3,497/3,499 SVC syncs and 718 timer syncs against 297 and 43 on the build before -- with NO FIOERRLC self fail-to-sync in the whole run; mmu1 and mmu2 listeners delivered 26,642 and 324,655 words with 0 gone past unread (against 21,748 and 31,252 unread before); the only votes are GPC2's at 497.173 s, cleared at 497.535 s, and both computers enter FCMSFAIL from 1b15c with R3=00008000.  THE NEXT STOP: A DELIBERATE DISSOLUTION ABOUT 31 S AFTER FORMATION, DETERMINISTIC. Runs ci-short-0 (OPS at 450 s) and ci-std-0 (OPS at 600 s): the set forms at 465.59 / 615.35 s and holds in lockstep -- about 1,050 SVC and 225 timer syncs per 10 s on both -- until 497.17 / 646.77 s (31.6 / 31.4 s), when BOTH computers enter FCMSFAIL within 25 us of each other from 1b15c = FPMSVC+0022 (DASS_G2 map 1b13a..1b163), the SVC handler, with R3=00008000; GPC2 votes and clears 0.36 s later; afterwards only SSIP and I/O-complete syncs continue (about 62 per 10 s each), i.e. a common set, no redundant set. So flight software on both computers asks for it at the same moment. NOT DUAL MASS-MEMORY COMMANDERS, although it first looked like it: during the set GPC1 and GPC2 each arm BCE18 and BCE19 receives with xmit=1, but at DIFFERENT addresses on the same bus (GPC1 bus 18 at 1cbb2/1d140/..., GPC2 at 1cbc6/1d13c/...) -- FIOMGDSP's FIOMMCMD picks commander or listener per I/O from the NBAT (commander 'IF (CH,R5,NE,TFCMID)' else listener, 'LA R2,2(R2) INCREMENT PTR TO LSTNR'), and FIOMMUPG's listener entries sit past the commander's #CMDI/#CMD ('#DLYI 0 *ALIGNMENT FOR LISTENER PROGRAM'), so a listener only receives and its transmitter being enabled does not matter. ARXRSBUS's ARX_STRNG_AND_BUS_ASGN reassigns only strings 1-4 and buses 10-13, never 18-19, consistent with that. The vehicle's '1,690,785 command(s) from a second computer while the device still owed words' on bus 19 is MISLEADING: the router passes 'in transfer' for every service, so a listener's READS count as clashes; with listeners now receiving (mmu2 listeners 324,655 words, 0 unread) the count is expected. The clash count is being corrected to commands only. Runs xe-short-0 (YAGPC_XMITENA_TRACE, RTRACE on FPMSVC entry 1b13a and FCMSFAIL) will show the transmitter history and the SVC that dissolves the set.
  - addrs `90c4-90c4,1cc2e-1cc2e,1ccae-1ccae,19dc7-19dc7` &middot; csects `FIOPRMPG,FIOERRLC,FCMSFAIL` &middot; instrs `#CMDI,#CMD,#RDLI,#MIN` &middot; procs `BCE20,BCE21,BCE22` &middot; env `YAGPC_TIMEOUT_TRACE,YAGPC_WIRE_HOLD_BUSES,YAGPC_REPEATTRACE` &middot; files `src/iop_bce_instr.c` &middot; symptom `redundant set breaks,self fail to sync,listener IUAR zero,Listen Mode waits forever,MTU listener,MSC single look busy` &middot; doc `BCE POO 3.3.1 #CMDI/#CMD; FIOPRMPG.asm listener code` &middot; run `w7-short-0,dh-short-0,dh-regress`
- **#139** *(emulator)* THE REDUNDANT SET DISSOLVES 31 S INTO THE OPS 2 TRANSITION BECAUSE GPC1'S MASS-MEMORY LISTENER ERROR-TERMINATES ON STALE WORDS LEFT IN ITS TAP, AN OVERLAY ERROR IS RECORDED FOR GPC1, AND ARCGPC DROPS IT. The listener tap discarded only paced (streamed) words on a new command; an unread status reply and its command echo stayed queued, the next receive took them as its trigger and first word, the new command's echo then arrived mid-receive, and a command-sync word mid-receive error-terminates the BCE.
  - evidence: Run mg-short-0 (YAGPC_ERRTERM_TRACE=18,19, RECV TIMEOUT from 440 s, RTRACE on FIOMGSNC entry 1a54c with the error code in R4 and FCMSFAIL). During the set: FIOMGSNC with error code 0080 and, 1 ms later, ERRTERM of the LISTENER's 2-word status receive at pc 1cbc6 with left=1 -- GPC2 on BCE18 at 467.180 and 468.641 s (IOQE 90c4, 90b2), GPC1 on BCE19 at 470.093, 471.560, 484.153 and 496.948 s (IOQE 90d6); NO receive time-out on either bus; then both computers enter FCMSFAIL from FPMSVC+0022 with R3=00008000 at 497.1727 s. Run ow-short-0 (YAGPC_WATCHHW=23fc-241b): at 497.1726/497.1727 s FCMSFAIL+77 (nia 19503) clears CZ2B_RS on both computers, and at 498.0946 s GPC2 alone stores CURRENT_OPS=2 (nia 451e5), which GPC1 then receives over the ICC -- GPC1 was dropped, GPC2 completed the transition. SOURCE: FIOMGSNC puts the erring computer's ID in an overlay transaction's status word ('PLACE IN THE TRANSACTION STATUS WORD THE IDENTITY OF THE GPC EXPERIENCING THE ERROR IF AN OVERLAY I/O REQUEST'); ARCGPC's ARC_MEM_OVL folds ARC_OVL_TSW into ARC_OVL_ERR and ARC_ERR_FLAGS, and 'DROP ERROR GPC'S FROM CURRENT RS' calls ARC_GPC_DROP, whose RSFAIL is SVC 39 (R4=0027 at FCMSFAIL entry). FIOMMUPG's listener entry for the status read receives only ('#RDLI 1'), so its trigger is the commander's #CMDI echo. RULED OUT ON THE WAY: dual mass-memory commanders (listeners run receive-only programs at different addresses; the 1.69 M 'clashes' were listener reads, now no longer counted), GPC-to-GPC overlay (ARC_SRC_GPC 1 and 2 are never set), transmitter management (YAGPC_XMITENA_TRACE: FCMBUSCM's enable/disable pairs at formation arrive and apply). FIX UNDER TEST: clear each listener's whole tap on every new command, then push the new echo; runs tc-regress, tc-short-0, tc-std-0. CORRECTION -- STALE LEFTOVERS WERE NOT THE CAUSE. The fix above (clear each listener's whole tap on every new command) changed nothing: run tc-short-0 shows the same six ERRTERMs at the same instants (GPC2 BCE18 467.180/468.641 s, GPC1 BCE19 470.097/471.561/484.154/496.947 s, all pc 1cbc6 left=1), mmu listeners now only 2 and 7 words 'gone past unread', and the same drop at 497.1726 s; its regression gate tc-regress passes. BETTER READING: GPC1's own clock is past YAGPC_TIMEOUT_TRACE_FROM=440 at these instants and no RECV TIMEOUT is logged, so the terminations are the command-sync path: a command-sync word after one data word. The listener's MM transmitter is enabled, so the receive runs in Command Mode (skip one echo, then data). FIOMMUPG's listener entries begin '#DLYI 0 *ALIGNMENT FOR LISTENER PROGRAM', and iop_bce_delay drains every word available while it runs; the tap released the echo and both status words at the instant of the commander's #CMDI, so the delay swallowed the echo and the first word, the #RDLI took the second word as its first, and the commander's next command echo then terminated it with one word left. On the wire the command takes 33 us and each reply word another 33 us, so a listener has reached its receive before they arrive. NEW FIX UNDER TEST: listener words carry wire times -- the echo is due a word time after the command or after the last word already on the wire, each reply word a word time after the one before, streamed words at their slots -- and the tap is no longer cleared on a command. SECOND CORRECTION -- THE WIRE-TIME READING ALSO CHANGED NOTHING. Build with listener tap words at wire times (echo a word time after the command or the last reply word, replies a word time apart, not-yet-sent stream words dropped on a new command): regression gate wtm-regress passes, but run wtm-short-0 shows the identical six terminations (GPC2 BCE18 467.181/468.641 s, GPC1 BCE19 470.094/471.561/484.153/496.931 s, pc 1cbc6, left=1, FIOMGSNC 0080), the same drop at 497.1726 s, and mmu listeners 4 and 17 words gone past unread. Two readings inferred from the listing have now failed; the next run (rw-short-0) traces every word a short receive on BCE18/19 takes, with its sync type and the receive's state, alongside the mass-memory model's command log, so the terminating word is observed rather than inferred. THREE COMPUTERS DO THE SAME. Run g3b-std-0 (rs30.sh: GPC3 IPLs from MM1 at 327 s, NBAT names GPCs 1-3, OPS 2 requested at 660 s; the YAGPC_DEUKEYS batch limit had to be raised from 16 to 64 for the seventeenth batch -- the OPS request -- to be parsed at all, run g3-std-0 having silently never requested it): a THREE-computer redundant set forms at 675.35 s and runs in lockstep -- 2,329/2,329/2,329 SVC and 450/450/450 timer syncs in the first 20 s, no votes -- until 707.093 s (31.7 s), when all three enter FCMSFAIL from FPMSVC+0022 with R3=00008000 at the same instant, and only SSIP syncs continue after. The same overlay-error dissolution as with two computers, so it is the single blocker for both. Regression gate bl- regress (batch limit 64, RECVWORD trace, wire-timed taps) matches on every event counter. OBSERVED, NOT INFERRED (runs rw-short-0 and rs- short-0, YAGPC_RECVWORD_TRACE=18,19 with the word's source): every terminating receive is the LISTENER's FIOM1PSR status read (pc 1cbc6), transmitter enabled so Command Mode; its first word is 00c6c6, data sync, src=LATCH; its second is the commander's BITE STATUS command 588000, command sync, src=bus, about 0.4-0.5 ms after the mass memory logged that command; a command-sync word after data then terminates it with left=1. The commander's receive (pc 1cbc0) gets both status words 000000 from the bus. The same latched 00c6c6 was also the only word of four earlier one-word listener receives 320-470 ms before, each src=latch. SOURCE: FIOMMUPG's receiver program FIOM1PSR is only '#LBR@ / #RDLI 1 / #SST / #WAT' -- no delay -- while the transmitter program FIOM1RSR spends '#DLYI 0 *ALIGNMENT FOR LISTENER PROGRAM' and '#DLYI 0' before its #CMDI, so the listener is meant to be sampling before the command arrives and must find nothing stale. THE LATCH (commit 14a7b7581) is set only by iop_bce_delay from the last word a delay drained, for FCMBOOT's one-halfword '#RDLI CLEAR THE MIA BUFFER' after a delay in the same program; here it outlives the listener's previous program (whose closing delays drained a stream tail), its #WAT, and the MSC's start of FIOM1PSR. BCE POO on the buffer: 'once an entry is placed in the MIA buffer it stays there until either the BCE removes it or the MIA overwrites it', the BCE samples it only during a Receive Data or Wait for Index instruction, at most every 16.5 us. THIRD CORRECTION -- THE TERMINATIONS WERE A CONSEQUENCE, NOT THE CAUSE. With a started BCE's latched word cleared at @SIO (build tested in sl-regress: gate matches every event counter), run sl-short-0 shows NO BCE18/19 error terminations at all: the listener's FIOM1PSR status read now skips the commander's BITE STATUS command as its echo (sync=CMD, skipped=1) and takes both status words 000000 from the bus. But the set still dissolves at 497.1726 s, with FIOMGSNC error code 0080 on the same six transactions (GPC2 IOQE 90c4/90b2 at 467.180/468.640 s, GPC1 IOQE 90c4 at 470.096/471.560/484.151/496.946 s) -- and each FIOMGSNC error entry comes about 1.1 ms BEFORE that status read begins. FIOMGSNC 'WILL DISPATCH/START A READ TO CLEAR THE STATUS REGISTER AFTER EACH I/O REQUEST', so the status read is the recovery after an error on the PRECEDING mass-memory transaction, which fails on the listener only. 0080 is the transaction-error flag (FIOMCNTL FIOM0080 DC A(TIOQTRER)), set by FIOMNTR4 for a BCE NO-GO or by FIOMTOUT for an MSC time-out; with no error termination logged, the MSC's single look finding the listener's BCE still busy is the leading reading. FIOMMUPG notes 'COMMANDER DELAY = 1814 FOR TAPE REVERSAL. LISTENER DELAY = 1833', a listener 19 counts longer. Run ms2-short-0 traces the MSC's repeat arms, BCE18/19 busy/wait edges, receive time-outs and terminations to settle it.  WHAT 0080 IS, AND WHAT IT IS NOT. FIOMGSNC's error code 0080 is the mass-memory CHECKSUM error (FIOMGMTR/FIOMGCMP/FIOMGERR 'FIOMMSUM EQU X''0080'' CHECKSUM ERROR'; FIOERRLC FIOMMCKS X'0080'). Run ms2-short-0 (REPEAT, BW, RECV ARM/TIMEOUT, ERRTERM on 18/19): GPC2's first 0080 at 467.180 s follows a complete overlay read on its listener BCE18 -- a chain of 1-word and N-word receives (98, 12, 1024, 330, 6656, 310, 308, 186, 6, 1024, 376, 160 words) ending with BCE18 idle 315 ms before the error, no time-out, no error termination, and no MSC single look on bus 18 in the 50 ms before it -- while GPC1, commanding the same transaction, reports 0000. So the LISTENER READS THE WHOLE SEGMENT AND IT FAILS ITS CHECKSUM; the MSC-look and still-busy readings are refuted. NOT AN OFF-BY-ONE AT SEGMENT BOUNDARIES: in rs-short-0 every 1-word receive between segments on bus 18 during that read takes the same latched 00c6c6 on BOTH computers within about 7 us of each other (GPC1 at pcs 1d146/1d14e/1d15a/..., GPC2 at 1d142/1d14a/1d156/...). Run fw-short-0 traces the first three words of every long receive on both computers to see where their data differ. THE LISTENER'S COPY STARTS LATE: THE MASS MEMORY STREAMS BEFORE THE LISTENER ARMS. Run fw-short-0 (first three words of every long receive): at 466.2105 s the commander GPC1's 98-word overlay receive (FIOMMUPG FIOMNNRD #RDL, pc 1d140) takes 009614 000e90 008fd2 ...; the listener GPC2 (pc 1d13c, FIOM1RRD) arms 444 us later and takes 000e90 009bd4 000e90 -- about thirteen words in, i.e. what the wire carried after its arm. The model queued a READ's first data word at the READ command's own word time, so the head of each block went past during the listener's '#DLYI 1833' and iop_bce_delay discarded it, as a delaying BCE does; FIOMGCV's load-block checksum then failed (0080). FIOMMUPG states the constraint: 'COMMANDER DELAY = 1814 FOR TAPE REVERSAL. LISTENER DELAY = 1833 = TAPE REVERSAL + 19 FOR 2 CMDR'S CMD AND 17 FOR SYNC SKEW, PC1 ROLL-OVER, MSC AVAILABILITY, IOP ALIGNMENT SKEW, AND DISCRETE NOISE' -- at 16.5 us a tick the listener can arm about 560 us after the commander's READ, so no data word may be on the wire before then. FIX UNDER TEST: mmumodel.c read_latency_words(), the first data word of a READ waits 31 word times (~1 ms) after the command, YAGPC_MMU_READ_LATENCY_US overrides (0 = old behaviour); runs lt-regress, lt-short-0, lt-std-0, lt-g3-0. READ LATENCY ALONE DID NOT CURE IT; THE LATCH LOST THE COMMAND SYNC. Run lt-short-0 (1 ms read latency): the same 0080 errors (GPC2 own 310.183/311.643 s, GPC1 470.098 s). At 466.2159 s the listener GPC2's 98-word receive (pc 1d13c) took 5cbc00 src=latch sync=data -- the commander's READ command word, IUA 11, latched by iop_bce_delay during '#DLYI 1833' -- then 009614 000e90, the commander's first two words: one place late throughout. The latch kept the word but not its sync type, so Command Mode's one-echo discard (the listener's transmitter is enabled: FIOMMCMD chooses commander/listener per request from the NBAT and never touches transmitters, and FCMNINIT's FCMXMSK DC X'00003000' enables buses 18-19 at IPL) did not apply. FIX UNDER TEST: MIA.latchCmdSync, set in iop_bce_delay and restored by mia_get_data; runs lc-regress, lc-short-0, lc-std-0, lc-g3-0. LATCHED SYNC TYPE FIXED THE LISTENER; THE COMMANDER HAD THE SAME DEFECT FROM THE OTHER SIDE. Run lc-short-0: pairing every long receive by bus and count (fw2.py), 216 of 220 match through 560 s and the set survives to 518 s (was 497). All four mismatches are the COMMANDER's 9216-word receive (bus 18 GPC1 at 476.988/489.788 s, bus 19 GPC2 at 502.628/515.428 s) taking 00c6c6 src=latch -- the tail of the previous stream latched by its own '#DLYI 1814' -- before 00e9f3; the listener correctly took and discarded its latched READ echo. On the wire the commander's own '#CMD FIOCWE' echoes into its MIA and overwrites the one-word buffer; no device model echoes a command to its sender, so the stale word survived. FIX UNDER TEST: mia_xmit_cmd latches the transmitted command marked command sync (Command Mode discards it; a bus word still overwrites it); runs le-regress, le-short-0, le-std-0, le-g3-0. A barrier hardening change (wait on progress, not 0.25 s of wall time) was written and set aside, untested, as scratchpad barrier-stall.patch: hardening comes after the set works. FIXED, all three together (read latency, latched sync type, latched echo). le-regress: single-GPC gate event counters ALL MATCH. le-short-0 (2 GPCs): set formed 465.6 s and held to the end of the run at 712.4 s, SVC/timer/SSIP sync counts equal within 3 per 20 s bin, no votes, no FCMSFAIL, no FIOMGSNC error, all 64 paired long MM receives identical, mmu1 listeners 162322 words delivered and 2 gone past unread, barrier 0 abandoned. le-std-0 (2 GPCs, OPS request at 600 s): set 615.4-958.7 s, no votes, no FCMSFAIL, no overlay error, identical MM counters. le-g3-0 (3 GPCs): set formed 663.8 s and held to 884.6 s, no votes, no FCMSFAIL, no overlay error; mmu1 listeners 184443 words gone past unread, most likely GPC3's own GPCIPL load from MM1 (400 s) streaming past GPC1's tap before any set exists -- mmu1 blocksRead 1181 = 749 + 432 -- not confirmed. Whether OPS 2 itself comes up after the load is checked separately (run le-img-0, display text). OPS 2 COMES UP ON THE SET, AND EVERY RUN ENDED ON THE HARNESS. le-img-0 (2 GPCs, YAGPC_DEUIMAGE=1, OPS request at 450 s wall): the DK6 image changes in the first report after the request, at about 487.8 s shared -- '0001 / /' and CODE go; '2011 / /', UNIV PTG, CUR/FUT, TRK 19 ROT 20 CNCL 21, ROLL PITCH YAW, CUR MNVR COMPL, START and DURATION arrive -- and UNIV PTG/2011 are present in every later report through 718.6 s while '0001' never returns: major mode 201 on screen. The set held from 465.6 s to the end of that run, no votes, no FCMSFAIL, no overlay error. Stop reasons (gpc.log) for le-short-0, le-std-0, le-g3-0 and le-img-0 are all 'interrupted (SIGINT)' on every computer -- the harness's timeout -- with no invalid instruction or other ERROR, so none of them ended by crashing (the check #1's method note asks for). FOUR COMPUTERS. le-g4-0 (scratchpad rs40.sh: GPC4 IPLs from MM2 after GPC3; NBAT target set GPC 1-4, strings 1-4 to GPC 1-4, OPS request at 830 s wall): all four synced from 842.4 s to the end at 1045.4 s, SVC/timer/SSIP counts equal within 9 per 20 s bin, no votes, no FCMSFAIL, no FIOMGSNC error, all four computers stopped on 'interrupted (SIGINT)', barrier 0 abandoned (727 s held). mmu2 listeners 53360 gone past unread and mmu2 blocksRead 864 = 2 x 432: GPC4's own GPCIPL load streaming past GPC2's tap, as with GPC3 on MM1. A five-computer run (rs50.sh, GPC5 from MM1, target set 1-5, with YAGPC_DEUIMAGE) is le-g5-0.

- **#141** *(emulator)* THE CAM NEVER SHOWED A VOTE THE FLIGHT SOFTWARE SET AND CLEARED BETWEEN TWO SAMPLES, HAD NO DIAGONAL AT ALL, AND MISNAMED THE FAIL-DISCRETE REGISTER'S TOP BIT AS A SELF VOTE. The fail-vote register was published by sampling every 1024 steps, so a vote set and reset within ~2 ms never reached a panel; nothing published a computer's Computer Fail (the CAM diagonal); and discretes.c named register bit 0x10 'fail vote N+0 (self)'. SOURCES: IOP POO (IBM 6246556A) @SFD 'allows the MSC ACC bits 0,1,2,3, and 4 to be logically OR'ed with the Fail Discrete register ... Setting bit 0 will inhibit the output of the four fail discretes from bits 1-4', so 0x10 is the OUTPUT INHIBIT and 0x08..0x01 are fail discretes 1-4 = votes against N+1..N+4 (rotation confirmed by le-g5-0). RM status bit 15 VOTER FAIL LATCH 'in normal operation would indicate Computer Failure ... set ... when the hardware voter receives at least two of four inputs'; FCMSFAIL.asm lights it on purpose -- 'IF SELF IS ALONE AND NOT EXACTLY 2 GPC SET ... ISSUE GPC TEST VOTER COMMAND TO LIGHT SELF'S DIAGONAL' (LOAD TEST REGISTER 8810 with FCMFAIL F'3', 2 votes) -- and FCMSVC SVC 25 request 2 op 2 'SET GPC FAIL LIGHT' does the same. FIX: MSC.failDiscSeen records every @SFD bit and run.c publishes a sampled-away vote as both edges; new discrete register DISCRETES_REG_CFAIL (5, bit 31) carries the diagonal, composed FOR THE LAMP ONLY -- the rmVoterFail self test, or two non-inhibited votes against the computer (vehicle_votes_against), latched until the mode switch goes to HALT -- so nothing the flight software reads changes (fail votes between IOPs are still not fed to the emulated voter, whose latch would halt the processors); bit 0x10 renamed 'inhibit fail discrete outputs'. discretePanel: discretes.py knows REG_CFAIL; cam.py listens on all five GPC channels, un-rotates each row (0x10 blanks it), draws the diagonal from REG_CFAIL, asks each computer for both registers at start-up, takes messages one at a time and holds every lit lamp HOLD_MIN_S = 40 ms (the user's floor). VERIFIED: gate cm-regress ALL MATCH; offline cam.py test (cells 14/34/54/24/44, inhibit, VALUE, a 0.5 ms pulse held to 40 ms, a 60 ms pulse cleared at once); cf-short-0 (2 GPCs, YAGPC_MMU_READ_LATENCY_US=0 to bring back the #139 overlay drop): GPC1 0x08 lit cell 12 (held 40 ms), GPC2 0x01 lit cell 21, no diagonal; cf-g3-0 (3 GPCs, same fault): the set broke up with every computer alone at shared 698.61 s and cam.py lit 11, 22 and 33, out after 0.20-0.84 s. The two-votes-against path of the diagonal was not exercised.
